## SMB Sales Process

### Full Journey Map

```
FIRST CONTACT                    DISCOVERY CALL                 PROPOSAL
(Networking, referral,    →     (Free 30-45 min call;    →    (1-page proposal
 Facebook group,                 map pain points,               emailed same day;
 workshop attendee)              identify 2-3 automations)      fixed price, clear scope)
                                                                  │
    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │
    ▼
CLOSE                           BUILD                           RETAINER OFFER
(Decision in 1-3         →     (Deliver in 3-14 days;   →    (Offer $200-500/mo
 days; 50% upfront,              founder builds solo;           support retainer at
 50% on delivery)                show progress daily)           project completion)
```

**Detailed stage breakdown:**

| Stage | Duration | Activities | Conversion to Next Stage |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. First Contact** | Day 0 | Meet at networking event, receive referral, see post in Facebook group, attend workshop. Goal: schedule a discovery call. | 40-60% (to discovery call) |
| **2. Discovery Call** | Day 1-3 | Free 30-45 minute call (in person or Zoom). Walk through their operations. Identify automations. Give rough pricing. | 30-40% (to proposal — only qualified prospects) |
| **3. Proposal** | Same day as call | 1-page proposal: what you will build, what it does, timeline, fixed price, payment terms. No jargon. | 60-75% (of proposals convert; price is not a surprise) |
| **4. Close** | Day 1-3 after proposal | Brief follow-up call or text. Answer any questions. Send simple contract. Collect 50% deposit. | 85-90% (once verbal yes, most sign) |
| **5. Build & Deliver** | Day 3-14 | Build the automation. Show progress via short daily updates (text or email). Deliver, test together, train them. | N/A |
| **6. Retainer Offer** | At delivery | Offer ongoing support retainer ($200-500/mo). Frame as "insurance + improvements": monitoring, fixes, and monthly optimization. | 35-50% (of completed projects convert to retainer) |

**Total cycle: 1-3 days from discovery call to signed project. 3-14 days delivery. Often under 2 weeks from first contact to project completion.**

### SMB Objection Handling

#### Objection 1: "I can't afford it"

**Why they say it:** Genuine budget constraint, or sticker shock because they have never bought a technology service before.

**Response:**

> "I totally get that — $2,500 is a real investment for a small business. Let me ask you this: you mentioned you spend about 10 hours a week on [the manual process]. What's your time worth per hour? [Usually $50-150 for a business owner.] So that's $500-$1,500 a week you're spending on work a computer could do. The automation pays for itself in 2-4 weeks, and then it just keeps saving you money.
>
> If cash flow is tight right now, I can split it into two payments — half to start, half when it's done. And if it doesn't save you at least 5 hours a week, I'll work on it until it does."

**Key principle:** Reframe as ROI and payback period. Offer payment flexibility. Never discount without changing scope.

#### Objection 2: "My business is too small for AI"

**Why they say it:** They associate AI with big tech companies. They think AI is for enterprises, not plumbers.

**Response:**

> "That's actually the opposite of how it works. The big companies already have teams of people handling this stuff — they can afford to be inefficient. It's small businesses like yours where AI makes the biggest difference, because every hour you save goes straight to your bottom line.
>
> I just helped a [similar business type] automate their [similar process]. They're a [X]-person shop, just like you. It took me [X] days to build and now it saves them [X] hours a week. You don't need to be a tech company to use AI — you just need someone who knows how to build it for your specific business."

#### Objection 3: "I don't trust AI"

**Why they say it:** They have seen scary headlines, bad chatbot experiences, or they fundamentally distrust technology they do not understand.

**Response:**

> "I hear you, and honestly, a lot of the AI stuff in the news is hype. What I build is not Terminator stuff — it's practical automation. Think of it like a really smart assistant that handles your [scheduling / follow-ups / quoting / whatever their pain is].
>
> Here's what I'd suggest: let me show you a quick demo of what it would look like for your business. Takes 5 minutes. If it feels weird or wrong, no pressure at all. But most business owners, once they see it handling something that used to take them 2 hours in about 30 seconds, they get pretty excited."

**Key principle:** Demystify. Use analogies they understand. Offer a low-commitment demo.

#### Objection 4: "What if it breaks?"

**Why they say it:** Legitimate concern. Their business depends on things working. They have been burned by technology before.

**Response:**

> "Great question — reliability is the most important thing. Here's how I handle it: First, I build it with fallbacks. If the AI part ever has an issue, the system alerts you and falls back to the manual process — so your business never stops. Second, I test everything thoroughly before it goes live, and we do a trial run together. Third, I offer a support retainer [$200-500/mo] that includes monitoring, fixes, and updates. If something breaks, I fix it — usually within hours, not days.
>
> And honestly, the manual process you're using right now 'breaks' too — it's just that you're the one fixing it every time, at 3 hours a day. The automation is actually more reliable than doing it by hand."

### Proposal Template for SMB

**Structure (1 page, never longer):**

1. **What You Told Me** (2-3 sentences): Restate their problem in their words. "You're currently spending ~10 hours/week manually scheduling appointments and sending follow-up texts. Some leads fall through the cracks because you're too busy on a job to respond."

2. **What I'll Build** (bullet list, 3-5 items): Plain language. "An automated scheduling system that lets customers book online, sends confirmation texts, and follows up automatically if they don't show." No jargon.

3. **Timeline:** "I'll have this built and working within [X] days."

4. **Investment:** "$X,XXX — includes everything: build, testing, training, and 30 days of free support after launch."

5. **Payment Terms:** "50% ($X,XXX) to start. Remaining 50% when you're happy with the result."

6. **Next Steps:** "To get started, just sign below and send the deposit via [Zelle / Venmo / invoice]. I'll start building within 48 hours."

## Phase 1: First 90 Days Playbook (SMB-Focused)

### Overview

The first 90 days are about velocity and proof — generating enough local business connections to produce 8-15 paid SMB projects and enough case studies to begin the upmarket transition in Month 6.

### Week 1-2: Join Local Business Groups, Start Networking

**Goal:** Establish presence in 2-3 local business groups. Have 10+ conversations with small business owners. Schedule 3-5 discovery calls.

| Day | Activities | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| **Day 1-3** | Join Chamber of Commerce. Register for the next BNI visitor meeting. Research local trade associations (plumber/HVAC/contractor groups, restaurant owner groups). Set up Google Business Profile. Create simple 1-page website with discovery call booking link. | Chamber membership active, BNI visit scheduled, website live |
| **Day 4-7** | Attend first networking event. Reach out to personal network (friends, family, former colleagues) who own or know small business owners. Post in 2-3 local Facebook groups introducing yourself (not selling — asking questions about local business challenges). | 5+ conversations with business owners, 2+ discovery calls scheduled |
| **Day 8-14** | Attend second networking event. Follow up with everyone from the first event (email/text/LinkedIn within 24 hours). Conduct first 2-3 discovery calls. Send same-day proposals. Start publishing LinkedIn content (3 posts in first 2 weeks). | 3-5 discovery calls completed, 1-2 proposals sent, first LinkedIn posts published |

### Week 3-4: First Free Discovery Calls and Pilot Projects

**Goal:** Complete 5-8 discovery calls. Close 2-3 first projects. Begin delivery.

| Week | Activities | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| **Week 3** | Conduct 3-4 more discovery calls. Close first 1-2 projects from early proposals. Begin building first automation(s). Attend BNI chapter meeting (weekly commitment begins). Join a second networking group if the first is not producing. | 2 projects in progress, BNI membership active |
| **Week 4** | Deliver first project(s). Document results (hours saved, process improvement) for case studies. Ask first client for a referral. Send proposals from remaining discovery calls. Attend another networking event. Publish 3 LinkedIn posts. | First project delivered, first case study material gathered, first referral asked |

### Month 2: First 2-3 Paid Projects, Retainers Starting

**Goal:** 3-4 projects completed. 1-2 retainers signed. Case study portfolio begun. 8-12 discovery calls per month flowing.

| Week | Activities | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| **Week 5-6** | Deliver 2-3 projects. Offer retainer to every completed client. Begin second wave of projects from referrals and networking. Host first workshop or lunch-and-learn at a local venue. Formalize partnership conversations with 2-3 accountants/bookkeepers. Publish 3 posts/week on LinkedIn + first blog post. | 3-4 projects completed cumulative, 1-2 retainers active, workshop scheduled or delivered |
| **Week 7-8** | Continue project delivery (2-3 new projects). Retainer clients generating first recurring revenue. Document 2-3 case studies. Share results in networking groups (with permission). Begin outreach to local Facebook groups with helpful content. | 5-7 projects completed cumulative, 2+ retainers active, 2-3 case studies drafted |

### Month 3: 4-5 Projects, Growing Retainer Base, Case Studies Accumulating

**Goal:** 4-5 projects completed this month alone. 3-5 active retainers. 5-8 case studies ready. Discovery call pipeline self-sustaining at 10-12/month.

| Week | Activities | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| **Week 9-10** | Project delivery at steady pace. Retainer base growing. Partnerships producing first referrals. Workshop results: 3-5 discovery calls from the event. Review Month 1-3 metrics: which channels are producing? Double down on what works, cut what does not. | 10+ projects completed cumulative, 3-4 retainers active, channel mix validated |
| **Week 11-12** | Continue delivery. Prepare for Phase 2 transition: review SMB case studies and identify which ones demonstrate mid-market-relevant capabilities. Begin researching mid-market prospects. Draft first mid-market outreach messages. Publish 2-3 case study posts on LinkedIn (positioned for mid-market audience). | 12-15 projects completed cumulative, 4-5 retainers active, Phase 2 prep underway |

### Pilot Pricing for First 2-3 SMB Clients

The first 2-3 SMB projects can use "founding client" pricing to accelerate the portfolio:

| Service | Standard SMB Price | Founding Client Price | Conditions |
|---|---|---|---|
| Custom AI Build | $1,000-$5,000 | $500-$2,500 (50% discount) | Testimonial + permission to use as a case study + 2 referral introductions |
| Support Retainer | $200-$500/mo | $100-$250/mo (first 3 months) | 3-month minimum commitment |

**Positioning language:** "I'm building my local business practice and looking for 2-3 business owners who want to be my first clients. In exchange for letting me use your project as a case study and introducing me to a couple of people you know, I'll do the work at half price. You get a great deal, I get a reference — everyone wins."

| SMB projects delivered | 8-12 | 15+ |
| Retainers signed | 3-5 | 7+ |

## Phase 2: Moving Upmarket (Month 6+)

### Transition Strategy

By Month 6, the founder should have 15-20+ completed SMB projects, 5-8 documented case studies, 5-10 active retainers, and a proven delivery track record. This portfolio is the foundation for the mid-market push.

**The key positioning shift:** You are no longer a consultant with no track record asking for $50K. You are a consultant with 20+ completed AI projects, documented results, and references — who is now offering the same capability to larger organizations at a larger scale.

### Mid-Market Free Tier Funnel

The Free AI Opportunity Scan is the primary lead-generation mechanism for mid-market prospects. It converts cold or warm prospects into qualified pipeline by delivering genuine value before asking for money.

**Deliverables (as defined in Module 3):**
- Pre-call: 15-minute review of company website, product, and public information
- Live call: 45-60 minute structured conversation mapping current operations, pain points, and data assets
- Post-call: 1-2 page "AI Opportunity Brief" delivered within 48 hours — top 3 AI opportunities with rough impact sizing

**What makes it convert:**
The brief is designed to be *compelling but not actionable*. It surfaces specific, quantified opportunities (e.g., "Your document review process could be 70% faster with AI-assisted extraction, saving approximately $400K/year in associate time") but does not provide the architecture, data requirements, or implementation plan. The prospect sees the prize but needs the Diagnostic to reach it.

**The SMB portfolio advantage:** When presenting the brief, the founder can reference specific, relevant case studies: "I built a similar automation for a local [business type] and it reduced their [process] time by 70%. For your firm, the same approach at scale could save $X/year." This transforms the conversation from theoretical to proven.

**Cost to deliver:** 2-3 hours of founder time per scan ($400-600 in opportunity cost at $200/hr internal rate).

### Mid-Market Distribution Channels (Month 6+)

| Channel | Tactic | Expected Volume | Ramp Time |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LinkedIn content** (already running for 6 months) | Shift positioning toward mid-market audience. Publish SMB case studies reframed for mid-market relevance. Direct CTA to free AI Opportunity Scan. | 3-5 scan requests/month by Month 8-9 | Already warmed up |
| **Direct outreach** | Personalized messages to mid-market prospects referencing specific case studies | 2-4 scans/month from Month 7 | 4-6 weeks to first results |
| **Referrals from SMB clients** | SMB clients who know people at larger firms | 1-2 scans/month from Month 7+ | Organic; dependent on SMB relationships |
| **Partnerships (non-AI dev shops, management consultants)** | Partner firms refer mid-market clients for free scans | 1-2 scans/month from Month 8+ | 2-4 months to develop |
| **Events/Communities** | Offer free scans to attendees after industry talks | 0-2 scans/month (intermittent) | Requires speaking opportunities |

**Total target:** 4-8 free mid-market scans per month by Month 8-9, scaling to 8-12 per month by Month 12.

### Mid-Market Conversion Model

**Target:** 2-4 clients who have paid for at least an AI Diagnostic ($10-25K) within Months 6-12.

**Funnel math for Months 6-12:**

```
Month 6-8 (Ramp-Up):
  Free Scans conducted:     8-12 total (3-4/month)
  → Diagnostics closed:     2-3 (25-30% conversion)
  → Builds started:         0-1 (Diagnostics still in progress)

Month 9-12 (Momentum):
  Free Scans conducted:     16-24 total (4-6/month)
  → Diagnostics closed:     4-7 cumulative
  → Builds started:         2-4 (from earlier Diagnostics)
  → First retainer signed:  1-2
  → Revenue run rate:       $25-50K/month (mid-market + continuing SMB)

6-Month Totals (Months 6-12):
  Free Scans:               24-36
  Diagnostics sold:         4-7 ($60K-$140K)
  Builds sold:              2-4  ($100K-$280K)
  Support retainers:        1-3  ($6K-$36K annualized)
  Mid-Market Revenue:       $100K-$300K
```

### Mid-Market Target Personas

**Primary buyer personas for mid-market professional services:**

| Title | Company Profile | Why They Buy | How to Find Them |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Managing Partner / CEO** | Law firm, accounting firm, consulting firm (50-500 people) | Strategic vision, competitive pressure, P&L responsibility | LinkedIn search, industry directories, conference speaker lists |
| **COO / Chief Operating Officer** | Same | Operational efficiency mandate, cost reduction targets | LinkedIn, company websites |
| **Director of Innovation / Digital Transformation** | Mid-market financial services (200-2000 people) | Explicit AI/digital mandate, budget allocated | LinkedIn, press releases about digital initiatives |
| **VP/Head of Technology** | Professional services firms with tech teams | Technical evaluation authority, frustrated by vendor pitches | LinkedIn, tech meetups, GitHub |
| **Practice Group Leader** | Large law firms, Big 4 accounting | P&L for their practice, wants competitive advantage | LinkedIn, industry publications |

**Ideal Company Profile (ICP):**
- 50-500 professionals (sweet spot: 100-300)
- Professional services (law, accounting, consulting) or financial services
- Document-heavy or data-heavy operations
- No internal AI/ML team (may have 1-2 data analysts)
- Revenue $20M-$500M (budget exists but Big 4 consultancies are too expensive)
- Has mentioned AI, digital transformation, or innovation publicly

### Mid-Market Message Frameworks

**Warm outreach (leveraging SMB portfolio):**

> Subject: AI automation for [Company] — case studies from 20+ projects
>
> Hi [Name],
>
> I run an AI consulting practice that builds automation systems for businesses — I've delivered 20+ projects in the last 6 months across [industries], consistently reducing manual process time by 50-80%. [Specific case study: "For example, I built a system for a [business type] that automated their [process], saving them 15 hours/week."]
>
> I'm now working with larger firms to apply the same approach at scale. I noticed [Company] is [specific observation showing research]. I'd love to offer you a free 45-minute AI Opportunity Scan — I'll map your top 3 AI opportunities with rough impact estimates. No pitch, just analysis.
>
> Worth a conversation? [Calendly link]

**Cold outreach:**

> Subject: [Company] + AI: 20+ delivered projects, quick question
>
> Hi [Name],
>
> I help professional services firms automate their most time-intensive workflows using AI. In the last 6 months, I've delivered 20+ AI automation projects — from automated document processing to intelligent scheduling and client follow-up systems.
>
> I noticed [Company] is [specific observation]. I'm offering free AI Opportunity Scans for [industry] firms — a 45-minute call where I identify your top 3 AI opportunities with rough ROI estimates. No pitch, just analysis you can act on with or without me.
>
> Worth 45 minutes? [Calendly link]

### Mid-Market Sales Process

| Stage | Duration | Activities | Conversion to Next Stage |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. First Contact** | Day 1 | Outbound message, inbound from LinkedIn, referral, or event. Goal: secure a qualifying conversation. | 30-50% (to qualifying call) |
| **2. Discovery / Qualifying Call** | Day 2-7 | 15-minute call. Assess fit: company size, industry, pain points, decision-making authority, budget range. | 60-80% (to free scan) |
| **3. Free AI Opportunity Scan** | Day 7-14 | 45-60 minute structured call. Deliver AI Opportunity Brief within 48 hours. | 25-35% (to Diagnostic proposal) |
| **4. Follow-Up / Debrief** | Day 14-21 | Schedule debrief call. Review brief. Gauge interest. If interested, offer to send a Diagnostic proposal. | 70-85% (if they attend debrief) |
| **5. Proposal** | Day 21-28 | 1-3 page proposal: scope, deliverables, timeline, investment, ROI. Include relevant SMB case studies as proof. | 50-70% (of proposals convert) |
| **6. Close** | Day 28-42 | Address questions/objections. Negotiate scope or terms. Send SOW. Signed. | 85-90% (once verbal agreement) |

**Total cycle: 4-8 weeks from first contact to signed Diagnostic SOW.**

### Mid-Market Objection Handling

#### Objection 1: "We can do this in-house"

**Response framework:**

> "That's great that you have internal capability — it actually makes the engagement more effective because your team can maintain and extend whatever we build. Here's what I typically see: internal teams are excellent at running your core operations, but AI implementation is a specialized discipline that's evolving weekly. The average company that tries to build AI in-house takes 3-4x longer and spends 2-3x more than working with a specialist, because of the learning curve.
>
> What I'd suggest is this: let's start with the Diagnostic. If your team can execute the roadmap themselves, you'll have a concrete plan. If they need support on the complex initiatives, I can build those or work alongside your team to upskill them."

#### Objection 2: "It's too expensive"

**Response framework:**

> "I understand — this is a significant investment. The Free Scan identified approximately [$X] in annual savings. The Diagnostic investment of [$Y] is roughly [1-2%] of that annual opportunity.
>
> If budget is a constraint, we can start with a narrower-scope Diagnostic focused on your single highest-impact opportunity, which brings the investment to [$lower number]. Or we can structure payment across two months.
>
> What I would not recommend is waiting. Every month those manual processes continue costs approximately [$monthly cost]."

#### Objection 3: "We've tried AI before and it didn't work"

**Response framework:**

> "That's more common than you might think. Most failed AI projects fall into one of three categories: (1) poor use case selection, (2) adoption failure, or (3) point solutions that weren't integrated into workflows.
>
> My approach is different: the Diagnostic phase exists precisely to avoid these failures. And I can show you 20+ projects I've delivered in the last 6 months — real production systems, not demos. The pattern of what works and what doesn't is clear from that experience.
>
> Given your past experience, the Diagnostic is designed to be valuable on its own — a clear-eyed assessment informed by someone who's delivered dozens of AI projects."

#### Objection 4: "How do I know you can deliver as a one-person shop?"

**Response framework:**

> "Fair question. Let me answer it with numbers: I've delivered 20+ AI automation projects in the last 6 months, every one on time and on budget. I have [X] active support retainer clients. Here are [2-3 specific case studies with results].
>
> The one-person model is an advantage: you get the senior person doing the work, not a partner who sells and a junior team that delivers. I also work with vetted specialist contractors for larger Builds.
>
> And the Diagnostic is a contained 3-4 week engagement. You'll see my work quality firsthand before committing to a larger Build."

#### Objection 5: "We're not ready for AI yet"

**Response framework:**

> "I hear that a lot. Based on our Free Scan conversation, your [specific process] involves [specific manual work] that accounts for approximately [X hours/week]. That's not a readiness question — that's an opportunity question.
>
> 'Ready' for AI in 2026 means: you have data (even in spreadsheets and email), processes that follow patterns, and people whose time is better spent elsewhere. You checked all three boxes.
>
> That's exactly what the Diagnostic assesses — a realistic readiness picture and a phased approach that meets you where you are."

### Mid-Market Pilot Pricing (First 2-3 Clients)

Per Module 3's recommendation, the first 2-3 mid-market engagements use "founding client" pricing:

| Service | Standard Mid-Market Price | Founding Client Price | Conditions |
|---|---|---|---|
| AI Diagnostic | $10,000-20,000 | $7,500-14,000 (25-30% discount) | Testimonial + case study permission |
| AI Brain Build | $40,000-100,000 | $30,000-70,000 (25-30% discount) | Detailed case study with measurable results + referral introduction |
| AI Brain Support | $4,000-8,000/mo | $3,000-6,000/mo | 6-month minimum commitment |

**Positioning language:** "I've delivered 20+ AI automation projects for businesses of all sizes. I'm selectively partnering with 3 mid-market firms as founding clients in this segment. In exchange for being a design partner — allowing me to document results and making introductions to your network — you get preferential pricing and direct, senior-level attention."

## Brand & Positioning

### "AI Brain" Messaging Framework

The "AI Brain" metaphor is the core positioning concept. It differentiates from competitors who sell "AI tools," "chatbots," "automation," or "AI strategy." The brain metaphor communicates:

1. **Systemic, not point-solution:** A brain is interconnected — it is not a single tool but a network of capabilities
2. **Intelligent, not just automated:** A brain learns, adapts, and makes decisions
3. **Central, not peripheral:** A brain is at the core of operations — this is foundational infrastructure
4. **Persistent, not project-based:** A brain grows over time — this is an ongoing relationship

### Messaging by Audience

| Audience | Key Message | Language Register |
|---|---|---|
| **Local business owners (Phase 1)** | "I build custom automation that handles the tedious stuff — scheduling, follow-ups, quoting, invoicing — so you can focus on the work that actually makes you money." | Plain language, outcome-focused, relatable, no jargon |
| **C-suite / Managing Partners (Phase 2)** | "I build the AI brain of your firm — an integrated intelligence layer that turns your most time-consuming operations into your competitive advantage." | Strategic, outcome-focused, professional |
| **COO / Operations leaders (Phase 2)** | "Your team spends 60% of their time on work that AI can handle. I build the systems that give them that time back — not with chatbots, but with an integrated AI brain." | Operational, efficiency-focused, specific |
| **Technical evaluators (Phase 2)** | "I design and build production AI systems — agentic pipelines, RAG architectures, multi-model orchestration — integrated into your existing stack. Not demos. Production systems." | Technical, credibility-focused |
| **Referred prospects** | "[Referrer] mentioned you're dealing with [specific problem]. I helped them [specific result]. Happy to do a free 30-minute call to see if I can help — no pitch, just conversation." | Warm, low-pressure, social-proof-led |

### Channel-Specific Positioning

**LinkedIn headline (Phase 1):**
"I help local businesses automate the work that eats up their time | AI automation for plumbers, HVAC, salons, restaurants & trades"

**LinkedIn headline (Phase 2 transition):**
"I build the AI brain of businesses — from local trades to professional services firms | 20+ AI projects delivered"

**Website tagline:**
"Your business on autopilot. Built, not bought."

**Sub-headline:**
"Custom AI automation for small businesses. I handle the scheduling, follow-ups, quoting, and invoicing — so you can focus on what you do best."

**Email signature block:**

```
[Name]
AI Automation Consultant | [Company Name]
Custom AI for local businesses and professional services firms
→ Free Discovery Call: [link]
```

### Minimal Visual Identity

A solo consultant does not need a full brand identity in month 1. The minimum viable brand:

| Element | What You Need | Cost | Timeline |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Logo** | Clean wordmark (company name in a modern sans-serif font). No clip art. | $0-200 (Canva / simple freelancer) | Week 1 |
| **Color palette** | 2-3 colors: primary (navy or deep blue), accent (teal or emerald), and white. | $0 | Week 1 |
| **Typography** | One font family: Inter, Plus Jakarta Sans, or similar modern sans-serif. | $0 (Google Fonts) | Week 1 |
| **Website** | Single-page: hero (tagline + CTA for discovery call), services overview, about, contact. | $0-500 | Week 1-2 |
| **LinkedIn banner** | Professional banner with name, tagline, CTA. | $0 (Canva) | Week 1 |
| **Proposal template** | Clean 1-page template for SMB proposals. | $0 | Week 2 |
| **Business cards** | Simple cards for networking events. Include QR code to booking link. | $20-50 | Week 1 |

**Total investment for minimal viable brand: $20-750 and 8-16 hours of founder time.**

### Open-Source Contributions and Public Demos as Proof

Before having mid-market clients, credibility must be manufactured through visible evidence of expertise. Even during Phase 1, these assets support the Phase 2 transition:

| Activity | What to Create | Purpose | Timeline |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GitHub repos** | 2-3 open-source tools or templates (e.g., "Small business scheduling automation," "AI follow-up system for service businesses") | Demonstrate technical depth to mid-market technical evaluators | Month 1-3 |
| **Public demos** | Recorded screen walkthroughs of AI systems in action | Show prospects what automation looks like in practice | Month 1-3 |
| **Technical blog posts** | Deep-dive architecture posts ("How I Built an AI Scheduling System for a Local HVAC Company") | SEO + credibility with technical audiences | Ongoing |
| **Framework/tool releases** | A lightweight, free tool (e.g., "Should You Automate This? Calculator for Small Business Owners") | Lead magnet for discovery calls | Month 2-3 |

## Sources

1. SBA (Small Business Administration) — 33.2M small businesses in the US; fewer than 15% have adopted AI; local business networking effectiveness data.
2. BNI (Business Network International) — Referral networking ROI data; member businesses generate average of $2.8M in referral revenue annually across the network.
3. HubSpot, "Sales Email Statistics and Benchmarks" (2025) — cold email open rates, response rates, and follow-up sequence data for B2B outreach.
4. LinkedIn, "State of B2B Marketing" (2024-2025) — organic content reach, engagement benchmarks, and B2B lead generation statistics.
5. Hinge Research Center, "High Growth Study" (2024) — sales cycle benchmarks, channel effectiveness rankings, and conversion rate data for professional services firms.
6. RainGroup, "Top Performance in Sales Prospecting" (2024) — response rates for warm vs. cold outreach, multi-touch sequence effectiveness.
7. McKinsey Global Survey on AI (2024) — AI adoption rates by company size and industry, implementation gap data.
8. Gartner, "Market Guide for AI Consulting Services" (2024-2025) — AI consulting market size, buyer behavior, and competitive landscape data.
9. SCORE "Marketing for Small Business" — Local marketing channel effectiveness, referral program design, and small business buying behavior.
10. U.S. Chamber of Commerce, "Small Business Impact Report" (2025) — Local business networking ROI, member engagement data.
11. Module 1: Market & Demand Analysis — AI adoption landscape, target segment data, pain point mapping.
12. Module 2: Competitive Landscape — competitor positioning, market gaps, differentiation strategy.
13. Module 3: Service & Pricing Model — service ladder, pricing, conversion targets, margin analysis, pilot pricing, and upsell logic.
14. Module 8: SMB/Local Business Lane — SMB project sizing, retainer model, delivery timelines, local go-to-market channels.

# Module 6: Financial Model

## Executive Summary

- **Break-even (fixed costs + minimum personal draw) requires approximately $5,400-$8,600 in monthly revenue.** Under the base case scenario, this is achieved by Month 1; under the conservative scenario, by Month 3; and under the optimistic scenario, by Month 1. The solo + AI agents model (zero contractors) keeps variable costs near zero, meaning nearly every revenue dollar contributes to covering fixed costs and personal draw.
- **The base case projects $285,100 in total Year 1 revenue with cumulative net cash flow of +$176K by Month 12**, driven by SMB project volume in Months 1-6 and a transition to combined SMB + mid-market revenue in Months 7-12. The elimination of contractor variable costs (previously 23% of mid-market Build revenue) adds approximately $29K in cash flow versus the contractor-based model.
- **The SMB-first strategy dramatically reduces cash flow risk in Months 1-5.** Unlike the mid-market-first approach where revenue might not materialize until Month 3-4, SMB projects generate revenue within weeks of launch. A starting runway of $30-50K is sufficient under all three scenarios, with the conservative scenario never dipping below $39K cash on hand. AI coding agents ($100-300/month) replace contractor costs entirely, keeping the cost structure exceptionally lean.

---

## Cost Structure

### Monthly Fixed Costs

These costs are incurred regardless of revenue and are compiled from the operational model (Module 4).

| Category | Lean (Mo 1-3) | Professional (Mo 4-6) | Growth (Mo 6+) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Google Workspace | $7 | $7 | $7 | Email, calendar, Drive |
| GitHub Pro | $4 | $4 | $4 | Code hosting |
| **AI Coding Agents** | **$70** | **$140** | **$220** | **Claude Code ($50-200), Cursor Pro ($20). Core delivery tools — replaces contractor costs.** |
| AI APIs (OpenAI + Anthropic) | $30 | $75 | $100 | Dev, demo, internal tools |
| Cloud infrastructure | $10 | $50 | $80 | Dev environments, demo hosting |
| Invoicing / Accounting | $0 | $19 | $33 | Wave (free) to FreshBooks |
| CRM | $0 | $0 | $14 | HubSpot free to Pipedrive |
| Project management (Notion/Linear) | $0 | $10 | $10 | Free tiers initially |
| Communication (Slack/Zoom) | $0 | $13 | $13 | Free tier Slack; Zoom Pro only if needed |
| LangSmith (AI observability) | $0 | $0 | $39 | Free tier initially |
| E&O Insurance | $100 | $150 | $200 | Professional liability |
| General Liability Insurance | $30 | $30 | $50 | Often bundled with E&O |
| Domain + hosting | $15 | $15 | $20 | Website, basic hosting |
| Legal (amortized) | $42 | $42 | $42 | $500 templates over 12 months |
| Registered agent / LLC | $15 | $15 | $15 | Entity maintenance |
| 1Password | $8 | $8 | $8 | Security hygiene |
| Backblaze backup | $7 | $7 | $7 | Machine backup |
| LinkedIn Premium | $0 | $60 | $60 | Lead research (from Mo 4) |
| QuickBooks | $0 | $15 | $15 | Bookkeeping |
| **Total Fixed Costs** | **$338** | **$660** | **$937** |
| **With buffer (+15%)** | **$389** | **$759** | **$1,078** |

**For modeling purposes, we use the following monthly fixed cost schedule:**
- Months 1-3: **$400/mo** (lean launch with modest buffer)
- Months 4-6: **$750/mo** (professional baseline — includes AI coding agents at moderate usage)
- Months 7-12: **$1,100/mo** (growth operations — includes AI coding agents at full usage)

**Note on AI coding agent costs:** The "AI Coding Agents" line item ($70-$220/month) replaces what would traditionally be contractor costs of $5,000-$20,000/month. This is the single biggest structural advantage of the solo + AI agents model — the cost of the "development team" is a fixed $100-300/month instead of a variable $0-$20,000/month that scales with project volume.

### Personal Draw

| Level | Monthly Amount | Annual | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Minimum survival** | $5,000 | $60,000 | Covers basic living expenses; requires existing savings buffer |
| **Target comfortable** | $10,000 | $120,000 | Sustainable long-term; approximate parity with senior employee compensation |
| **Stretch (Month 9+)** | $15,000 | $180,000 | Reward for business traction; enables savings rebuilding |

**For modeling, personal draw schedule:**
- Months 1-3: $5,000/mo (minimum — preserving runway)
- Months 4-6: $5,000/mo conservative / $7,500 base / $7,500 optimistic
- Months 7-12: $7,500 conservative / $10,000 base / $12,500 optimistic

### Variable Costs per Project Type

Variable costs are incurred only when delivering client work. In the solo founder + AI agents model, variable costs are dramatically lower than a contractor-based model because AI coding agent subscriptions are a fixed monthly cost (captured in fixed costs above), not a per-project variable cost. The only true variable costs are API/compute charges for LLM calls and cloud infrastructure used during project delivery.

**SMB Project Variable Costs:**

| Service | API/LLM Cost | Infra/Compute Cost | Total Variable Cost | As % of Revenue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Free Discovery Call (SMB)** | $0 | $0 | $0 | N/A (no revenue) |
| **SMB Custom Build ($1-5K)** | $5-15 | $0-5 | $5-20 | 0.2-0.8% of $2,500 avg |
| **SMB Support Retainer (/mo)** | $5-10 | $0-5 | $5-15 | 2-5% of $300/mo avg |

**Mid-Market Project Variable Costs (Phase 2, Month 6+):**

| Service | API/LLM Cost | Infra/Compute Cost | Total Variable Cost | As % of Revenue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Free AI Opportunity Scan** | $0 | $0 | $0 | N/A (no revenue) |
| **AI Diagnostic (Mid-Market)** | $50 | $50 | $100 | 0.7% of $15,000 |
| **AI Brain Build (Mid-Market, solo + AI agents)** | $200-400 | $100-200 | $300-600 | 0.4-0.9% of $70,000 |
| **AI Strategy & Adoption (Mid-Market)** | $50 | $50 | $100 | 0.4% of $25,000 |
| **AI Brain Support (Mid-Market, /mo)** | $50-100 | $50-100 | $100-200 | 1.7-3.3% of $6,000/mo |

**Blended variable cost assumptions for modeling:**
- SMB builds: **0.5% of revenue** (near-zero; just API costs for LLM calls during development)
- SMB retainers: **3% of revenue** (minimal ongoing API/monitoring costs)
- Mid-market Diagnostics: **0.7% of revenue** (primarily founder-delivered, minimal compute)
- Mid-market Builds: **0.7% of revenue** (solo + AI agent delivery; no contractor costs — a dramatic reduction from the 23% contractor-augmented model)
- Mid-market Strategy: **0.4% of revenue**
- Mid-market Support retainers: **2.5% of revenue**

**Margin impact of eliminating contractors:**

The single most significant financial difference in the solo + AI agents model is the elimination of contractor variable costs on mid-market Builds. Under the previous contractor-augmented model, a $70,000 Build carried ~$16,200 in variable costs (23% of revenue). Under the solo + AI agents model, the same Build carries ~$300-600 in variable costs (<1% of revenue). This transforms mid-market Build gross margins from **~77% to ~99%** on a direct cost basis. Even accounting for the founder's time at $200/hr opportunity cost (which is not a cash cost), the effective gross margin is dramatically higher.

For SMB builds, the change is smaller but still meaningful: variable costs drop from 3% to 0.5%, pushing SMB gross margins from 85-90% to 92-95%+ on a direct cost basis.

### Total Monthly Burn (Pre-Revenue)

| Component | Months 1-3 | Months 4-6 | Months 7-12 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fixed costs (incl. AI agent subs) | $400 | $750 | $1,100 |
| Personal draw (minimum) | $5,000 | $5,000 | $7,500 |
| **Total monthly burn** | **$5,400** | **$5,750** | **$8,600** |

**Note:** Monthly burn is marginally higher than a hypothetical zero-tooling model because of AI agent subscriptions ($70-$220/month), but this is trivially small compared to the revenue-generating capacity those tools provide. The absence of contractor variable costs means that actual cash outflow during delivery months is far lower than a contractor-based model — a $70K Build no longer triggers $15K+ in contractor payments.

## Revenue Model

### SMB Revenue Assumptions (Phase 1 — Months 1-6, continuing through Month 12)

| Parameter | Value | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Average project size | $2,500 | Mix of $1-5K projects; most cluster around $2-3K |
| SMB support retainer | $300/mo average | Range $200-$500/mo; most local businesses start at $200-$300 |
| Discovery calls per month | 8-12 | Local networking, word of mouth, chamber events |
| Discovery → project conversion | 30-40% | Lower friction than mid-market; price point is approachable |
| Average delivery time | 1-3 days | Simple automations; founder + AI agents deliver 2-3x faster than solo |
| Gross margin on builds | 85-92% | Solo + AI agent delivery, near-zero variable costs (just API calls ~$5-20/project) |
| Retainer conversion | 40-50% | Not all SMB clients need ongoing support |

**SMB project ramp (base case):**
- Month 1: 2-3 projects
- Month 2: 3-4 projects
- Month 3: 4-5 projects
- Month 4-6: 5-7 projects/month (steady state)
- Month 7-12: 3-5 projects/month (reduced as mid-market work begins)

### Mid-Market Revenue Assumptions (Phase 2 — Months 6-12)

Based on Module 3 recommended pricing, using midpoints for modeling:

| Service | Price Range | Model Midpoint | Typical Duration |
|---|---|---|---|
| Free AI Opportunity Scan | $0 | $0 | 1 week |
| AI Diagnostic | $10,000-$20,000 | $15,000 | 3-4 weeks |
| AI Brain Build | $40,000-$100,000 | $70,000 | 6-8 weeks |
| AI Strategy & Adoption | $15,000-$35,000 | $20,000 | 3-4 weeks |
| AI Brain Support | $4,000-$8,000/mo | $6,000/mo | Ongoing |

**Note on early mid-market pricing:** First 2-3 mid-market clients receive "founding client" pricing at a 25% discount. This reduces the effective Diagnostic to ~$11,250 and Build to ~$52,500 for initial engagements.

## 12-Month Projections

### Assumptions Common to All Scenarios

- **Starting runway:** $40,000 (midpoint of $30-50K range)
- **SMB-first phasing:** Months 1-6 are SMB-only or SMB-primary; mid-market enters in Phase 2
- **SMB revenue recognized:** In the month the project is delivered (3-14 day delivery cycles)
- **SMB retainer revenue:** Recognized monthly, starting the month after project delivery
- **Mid-market Diagnostic revenue recognized:** In the month the project is delivered
- **Mid-market Build revenue recognized:** Split across 2 months (50/50)
- **Variable costs:** Recognized in the same month as revenue
- **First 2-3 mid-market clients:** Founding client pricing (25% discount on Diagnostics and Builds)

### Conservative Scenario

**Narrative:** Slow local networking start. The founder's local network is thin and takes time to build. Discovery call volume ramps slowly. SMB conversion rate is at the low end (30%). Mid-market does not appear until Month 8. No mid-market Build closes in Year 1, only Diagnostics.

- Month 1: First local networking events; 2 SMB projects from early contacts

- Month 4-6: Steady state at 4-5 SMB projects/month; retainer base growing
- Month 7-8: Begin mid-market outreach using SMB portfolio; first free scans
- Month 9-10: First mid-market Diagnostic closes ($11,250 founding-client price)
- Month 11-12: Second mid-market Diagnostic; SMB continues at reduced pace

- SMB retainer revenue: $27,900 (6%)
- Mid-market Diagnostics: 3 x avg $18,750 = $56,250 (12%)
- Mid-market Builds: 3 x avg $64,167 = $192,500 (42%)
- Mid-market Strategy (bundled): $20,000 (4%)
- Mid-market Retainers: $24,000 (5%)
- Enterprise premium: Included in mid-market totals above

## Break-Even Analysis

### Monthly Break-Even Calculation

Break-even is the point at which monthly revenue covers all costs including the founder's personal draw.

**Minimum break-even (Months 1-3):**
- Fixed costs: $400
- Personal draw (minimum): $5,000
- **Total: $5,400/mo**

**Professional break-even (Months 4-6):**
- Fixed costs: $750 (includes AI coding agent subscriptions)
- Personal draw: $5,000-$7,500
- Variable costs (estimated at ~0.5-1% of revenue at break-even): ~$50-$80
- **Total: ~$5,800-$8,330/mo**

**Growth break-even (Months 7-12):**
- Fixed costs: $1,100 (includes AI coding agents at full usage)
- Personal draw (target): $10,000
- Variable costs at break-even: ~$100 (blended SMB + mid-market, near-zero per project)
- **Total: ~$11,200/mo**

**Note:** Break-even thresholds are marginally lower than a contractor-based model because variable costs are near-zero. The founder keeps ~99% of each revenue dollar as gross margin (before fixed costs and personal draw). This means every additional project drops nearly all its revenue straight to the bottom line.

### Break-Even by SMB Client Configuration (Phase 1)

How many SMB projects and retainers are needed to reach break-even at the $5,400/mo minimum:

| Configuration | Monthly Revenue | Meets Break-Even? |
|---|---|---|
| 2 SMB projects ($2,500 avg) only | $5,000 | No — covers 93% |
| 2 SMB projects + 2 retainers ($300/mo) | $5,600 | Yes — 104% |
| 3 SMB projects only | $7,500 | Yes — 139% |
| 4 SMB projects + 3 retainers | $10,900 | Yes — 202% |
| 5 SMB projects + 5 retainers | $14,000 | Yes — 259% |
| **Target steady state: 5-7 projects + 8-10 retainers** | **$15,400-$20,500** | **Yes — 285-380%** |

### Break-Even by Mixed Configuration (Phase 2, Months 7-12)

At the $11,750/mo growth break-even:

| Configuration | Monthly Revenue | Meets Break-Even? |
|---|---|---|
| 4 SMB projects + 8 SMB retainers only | $12,400 | Yes — 105% |
| 3 SMB projects + 8 SMB retainers + 1 mid-market retainer | $15,900 | Yes — 135% |
| 3 SMB projects + 10 SMB retainers + 1 mid-market Diagnostic/quarter | $15,500 | Yes — 132% |
| **Target: 3-4 SMB projects + 10 retainers + 1 Build (over 2 mo)** | **$47,400-$52,000/mo** | **Yes — 400%+** |

### Break-Even Month by Scenario

| Scenario | First Revenue | First Month Revenue > Costs | Sustained (3+ consecutive months) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | Month 1 | Month 3 ($8,100 > $5,400) | Month 3 |
| Base Case | Month 1 | Month 1 ($7,500 > $5,400) | Month 1 |
| Optimistic | Month 1 | Month 1 ($10,000 > $5,400) | Month 1 |

### Cumulative Break-Even (Recoup All Prior Losses)

The point at which cumulative net cash flow turns positive (meaning all prior months' losses have been recovered):

| Scenario | Cumulative Break-Even Month | Total Investment Before Positive |
|---|---|---|
| Conservative | Month 3 (cumulative: +$1,603) | $824 |
| Base Case | Month 1 (cumulative: +$1,875) | $0 |
| Optimistic | Month 1 (cumulative: +$4,300) | $0 |

**Key advantage of SMB-first:** Even the conservative scenario reaches cumulative break-even by Month 3, compared to Month 5 in the previous mid-market-first model. The cash gap risk is nearly eliminated.

### Danger Zone Identification

The "danger zone" is when cash on hand drops below 2 months of minimum burn ($5,400 x 2 = $10,800):

| Scenario | Enters Danger Zone? | Lowest Point | Months in Danger Zone |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | **No** — lowest is $39,432 (Month 2) | $39,432 | 0 |
| Base Case | **No** — cash grows from Month 1 | $40,000 (start) | 0 |
| Optimistic | **No** — cash grows from Month 1 | $40,000 (start) | 0 |

**What if starting runway is only $30,000?**

| Scenario | Enters Danger Zone? | Lowest Point | Risk Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | **No** — lowest is $29,432 (Month 2) | $29,432 | Low |
| Base Case | **No** — grows from Month 1 | $32,051 | Negligible |
| Optimistic | **No** | $34,538+ always | Negligible |

**What if starting runway is only $20,000?**

| Scenario | Enters Danger Zone? | Lowest Point | Risk Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | **No** — lowest is $19,432 (Month 2) | $19,432 | Low-Medium |
| Base Case | **No** — grows from Month 1 | $22,051 | Low |
| Optimistic | **No** | $24,538+ always | Negligible |

**Minimum recommended runway: $30,000.** The SMB-first model is dramatically less capital-intensive than the mid-market-first approach. Even with only $20K, the conservative scenario maintains $19K+ at the lowest point — enough for 3.5 months of minimum burn. Starting with $40,000 is preferred for psychological comfort and to cover any unexpected delays, but the financial risk is low.

### Cash Flow Risk Factors

| Risk | Impact | Probability | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| SMB demand lower than expected (50% of plan) | Extends ramp by 2-3 months; $5K-$10K less revenue in Months 1-3 | Medium (25%) | Expand geographic radius; add digital channels (Facebook groups, Nextdoor); offer first 2-3 projects at steep discount to build references |
| SMB clients slow to pay | One-time $2-5K timing gap per late payer | Low (15%) | Require 50% upfront; remainder on delivery; use simple invoicing with online payment |
| Mid-market Phase 2 delayed by 3+ months | $30-60K less revenue in Year 1; business remains viable on SMB alone | Medium (30%) | Continue SMB at full pace; do not reduce SMB volume prematurely; mid-market is upside, not survival |
| SMB retainer churn higher than expected (>10%/mo) | $1K-$2K/mo less MRR than modeled | Medium (25%) | Demonstrate value monthly; offer retainer "pause" rather than cancel; check in proactively |
| Scope creep on SMB projects | $500-$1,000 in unreimbursed time per project | High (35%) | Fixed-price proposals with clear scope; change order process even at SMB level; learn to say no |
| Two mid-market projects overlap (capacity risk) | Overcommitment, quality risk | Low (15%) | Stagger start dates; temporarily reduce SMB volume; consider this as first hire trigger if recurring |

## Key Metrics to Track

### 1. Discovery Call Volume (SMB — Phase 1 Primary Metric)

**What it is:** Number of free 30-45 minute discovery calls conducted per month with local business owners.

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| Discovery calls per month | 10-12 | Below 6 (lead generation channels underperforming) |
| Discovery call sources | 3+ sources (networking, referrals, digital) | Single-source dependency |
| Discovery → project conversion | 35%+ | Below 25% (qualification or pitch issue) |

**Action triggers:**
- If calls per month drop below 6: Attend more networking events; post in local Facebook groups; ask every completed client for 2 referrals
- If conversion drops below 25%: Review discovery call structure; check if pricing is causing friction; assess whether leads are qualified

### 2. SMB Project Velocity

**What it is:** Number of SMB projects completed per month and average delivery time.

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| Projects completed per month | 5-7 (steady state) | Below 3 (demand or capacity issue) |
| Average delivery time | 3-7 days | Above 14 days (scope creep or overcommitment) |
| Average project revenue | $2,500+ | Below $1,500 (gravitating toward lowest-price work) |

### 3. Pipeline Value (Blended — Phase 2)

**What it is:** Total dollar value of all prospects at each stage of the funnel, weighted by conversion probability.

**How to calculate (Phase 2):**
- Free Scans completed x 28% conversion x $15,000 avg Diagnostic = Weighted Diagnostic pipeline
- Active Diagnostics x 55% conversion x $70,000 avg Build = Weighted Build pipeline
- Sum all weighted values

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| Weighted pipeline value | 3x+ next quarter's mid-market revenue target | Less than 1.5x next quarter's target |
| Pipeline-to-close ratio | Closing 25%+ of weighted pipeline | Closing less than 15% |
| Time in pipeline stage | Deals moving within 4-6 weeks | Deals stalling for 8+ weeks |

### 4. Monthly Recurring Revenue (MRR)

**What it is:** Total retainer revenue committed on a monthly basis (SMB + mid-market combined). The single most important metric for business stability.

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| MRR absolute value | $3,000+/mo (10+ SMB retainers) by Month 6 | Below $1,500/mo by Month 6 |
| MRR from SMB retainers | Adding 1-2 new retainers per month | Flat or declining for 2+ consecutive months |
| MRR churn (SMB) | Less than 8%/mo | Above 12%/mo (retainers churning within 2-3 months) |
| Combined MRR by Month 12 | $9,000+ (base case target) | Below $5,000 |

**Target trajectory:**
- Month 3: $600 MRR (2 SMB retainers)
- Month 6: $1,800-$3,000 MRR (6-10 SMB retainers)
- Month 9: $5,000-$9,000 MRR (SMB retainers + first mid-market retainer)
- Month 12: $9,000-$16,000 MRR (10-13 SMB retainers + 1-2 mid-market retainers)

### 5. Utilization Rate

**What it is:** Percentage of total working hours spent on billable client work (delivery + support).

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| Utilization rate | 60-75% | Below 50% (underbooked — activate sales) |
| Utilization rate | 60-75% | Above 80% (overbooked — pipeline building neglected) |
| Billable hours per week | 25-33 hrs (of 40-45 total) | Below 20 hrs (revenue risk) or above 36 hrs (burnout risk) |
| Non-billable allocation | 25-40% on sales, networking, admin, professional dev | Below 15% (business maintenance deferred) |

**The utilization trap:** High utilization (80%+) feels productive but is dangerous. It means no time for networking, discovery calls, or proposal writing. Two months at 80%+ utilization almost always precedes a revenue gap 2-3 months later when the pipeline is empty.

### 6. Gross Margin

**What it is:** (Revenue - Direct Costs) / Revenue, measured per engagement and as a blended monthly figure.

| Signal | "Good" | "Warning" |
|---|---|---|
| SMB build gross margin | 85%+ (solo + AI agents) | Below 80% (spending too many hours per project or scope creep) |
| SMB retainer gross margin | 90%+ | Below 80% (over-servicing retainer clients) |
| Mid-market Diagnostic gross margin | 85%+ (solo-delivered) | Below 75% (spending too many hours) |
| Mid-market Build gross margin | 80%+ (solo + AI agents) | Below 70% (scope creep or underpriced) |
| Blended monthly margin | 85%+ (direct cost basis) | Below 75% (something is structurally wrong — check for hidden costs) |
| Trend direction | Margins improving quarter-over-quarter (templatization effect) | Margins declining (scope creep, pricing erosion, AI API cost inflation) |

**Margin improvement levers:**
- Templatize common SMB builds: Reduce founder hours from 4-12 to 2-6 per project using AI-generated templates and reusable components, pushing effective margin toward 95%+
- Build reusable AI agent prompts/workflows: Common project types (chatbots, form automation, data pipelines) get standardized AI agent workflows that produce 80% of the deliverable in the first pass
- Bundle SMB retainers efficiently: Group check-ins and maintenance across multiple SMB clients into weekly batches
- Mid-market: Templatize Diagnostics, componentize Builds with reusable AI-generated modules, bundle Strategy with Builds

## Financial Decision Framework

### Monthly Financial Review Checklist

At the end of each month, evaluate these decision triggers:

| Metric | Threshold | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Cash on hand < 3 months burn | < $16,200-$25,500 | **Red alert.** Cut personal draw to minimum. Increase discovery call volume. Activate all network contacts. Offer discounted "quick-win" projects to generate immediate cash. |
| SMB projects < 3/month for 2+ consecutive months | — | **Demand problem.** Networking channels are not producing. Add new channels (digital, partnerships). Expand geographic radius. Consider offering first project free to build references. |
| MRR covers < 20% of break-even after Month 6 | MRR < $1,200 | **Retention problem.** SMB retainer conversion or retention is failing. Review post-project follow-up process. Are clients getting enough value from the retainer? |
| No mid-market Diagnostic closed by Month 9 | — | **Phase 2 problem.** Mid-market portfolio or outreach is not working. Evaluate: (a) increase scan volume, (b) strengthen case studies, (c) adjust positioning, or (d) commit to SMB-only model. |
| Blended margin below 60% for 2+ months (SMB phase) | < 60% | **Scope/pricing problem.** Audit the last 5 projects for scope creep. Raise prices on next proposal. Tighten project scope definitions. |
| Personal draw below $5,000/mo for 4+ months | — | **Viability question.** The business model is not working at current pace. Evaluate: (a) increase project volume, (b) raise prices, (c) reduce costs, or (d) consider hybrid employment while building. |
| Revenue above $10K/mo for 3+ consecutive months AND turning away work | > $10K/mo sustained | **First hire trigger.** Begin recruiting junior AI/full-stack developer ($4-6K/mo). See "When to Make First Hire" section below. |

### When to Increase Personal Draw

| Condition | Action |
|---|---|
| 3 consecutive months revenue > $12K + cash reserve > $40K | Increase draw to $7,500/mo |
| 3 consecutive months revenue > $25K + cash reserve > $60K | Increase draw to $10,000/mo |
| MRR alone covers 50%+ of break-even ($6K+ MRR) | Consider draw increase to $12,500/mo |
| Annual revenue on track for $300K+ with 60%+ margin | Consider draw of $15,000-$20,000/mo |

---

## When to Make the First Hire: Financial Analysis

### The Economic Logic

In the solo founder + AI agents model, the first hire is not about offloading contractor management — there are no contractors. It is about unlocking revenue capacity. The founder is at capacity (6-8 SMB projects/month + retainers), AI agents are being used at maximum effectiveness, and qualified leads are being turned away. The hire should directly increase revenue-generating capacity.

### Hire Profile: Junior AI/Full-Stack Developer

| Factor | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Monthly cost** | $4,000-$6,000 | Contractor at $25-$40/hr, or W-2 at $48-$72K/year. Start with contract to reduce risk. |
| **Ramp-up period** | 2-4 weeks | Junior learns the delivery model, tools, AI agent workflows, and quality standards |
| **Productive capacity** | 4-6 SMB builds/month | With AI agent assistance, handling simpler SMB projects independently after initial training |
| **Revenue capacity unlocked** | $10,000-$15,000/month | 4-6 builds x $2,500 avg |
| **Net contribution** | $4,000-$11,000/month | Revenue minus cost of hire |
| **Break-even for the hire** | 2-3 projects/month | The junior needs to deliver just 2-3 projects/month to cover their cost |

### Revenue Threshold Model

**When the hire is ROI-positive:**

| Monthly Revenue (Pre-Hire) | Hire Cost ($5K/mo) | Revenue Unlocked by Hire | New Total Revenue | Net Impact | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $10,000 | $5,000 | $5,000-$10,000 | $15,000-$20,000 | $0-$5,000 | **Too early.** Revenue cushion is thin. One slow month puts you underwater on the hire. |
| $15,000 | $5,000 | $8,000-$12,000 | $23,000-$27,000 | $3,000-$7,000 | **Marginal.** Works if pipeline is consistent, but cash reserve must be strong ($40K+). |
| $20,000 | $5,000 | $10,000-$15,000 | $30,000-$35,000 | $5,000-$10,000 | **Good.** Solid ROI. The hire pays for itself and adds meaningful revenue. |
| $25,000+ | $5,000 | $12,000-$15,000 | $37,000-$40,000 | $7,000-$10,000 | **Strong.** Clear ROI. The founder is also freed for higher-value mid-market work. |

**Recommended trigger: $10,000/month sustained revenue for 3+ consecutive months, AND the founder is turning away work or delaying mid-market opportunities due to SMB load.**

The range accounts for risk tolerance:
- $15K/month is the minimum where the hire math works (assuming consistent pipeline)
- $20K/month is the comfortable trigger (enough margin of safety)
- $25K/month is conservative — waiting this long means leaving money on the table, but the risk is minimal

### Impact on P&L

**Pre-hire P&L (at $20K/month revenue, solo + AI agents):**

| Line Item | Monthly | Annual |
|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $20,000 | $240,000 |
| Variable costs (~0.5%) | -$100 | -$1,200 |
| **Gross profit** | **$19,900** | **$238,800** |
| Fixed costs (incl. AI agents) | -$1,100 | -$13,200 |
| Personal draw | -$10,000 | -$120,000 |
| **Net cash flow** | **$8,800** | **$105,600** |
| **Effective margin** | **44%** | |

**Post-hire P&L (at $32K/month revenue, founder + junior + AI agents):**

| Line Item | Monthly | Annual |
|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $32,000 | $384,000 |
| Variable costs (~0.5%) | -$160 | -$1,920 |
| **Gross profit** | **$31,840** | **$382,080** |
| Fixed costs (incl. AI agents) | -$1,200 | -$14,400 |
| Junior developer cost | -$5,000 | -$60,000 |
| Personal draw | -$12,500 | -$150,000 |
| **Net cash flow** | **$13,140** | **$157,680** |
| **Effective margin** | **41%** | |

**Key takeaway:** The hire reduces effective margin by ~3 percentage points (from 44% to 41%) but increases absolute net cash flow by $4,340/month ($52,080/year). The founder also earns a higher personal draw ($12,500 vs. $10,000/month) while building a more scalable business.

### Decision Checklist

Before making the first hire, all of the following should be true:

- [ ] Monthly revenue has exceeded $15,000 for 3+ consecutive months
- [ ] The founder is at capacity (6-8 SMB projects/month) AND turning away qualified leads
- [ ] Cash reserve is at least $40,000 (4+ months of post-hire total burn)
- [ ] The SMB delivery model is templatized enough that a junior can follow it
- [ ] Mid-market opportunities are being delayed because the founder is consumed by SMB delivery
- [ ] The founder has documented delivery processes, quality checklists, and AI agent workflows that a junior can learn

**If any of these are not true, the hire is premature.** Continue with solo + AI agents until the conditions are met.

### Timeline Estimate by Scenario

| Scenario | Likely Hire Month | Revenue at Trigger | Cash Reserve at Trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | Month 12-15 | $10-15K/mo (may not hit trigger in Year 1) | $80K+ |
| Base Case | Month 9-12 | $20-25K/mo | $90-150K+ |
| Optimistic | Month 6-9 | $25-40K/mo | $90-130K+ |
