# Module 7: Risk Assessment & Go/No-Go Framework

## Executive Summary

- **The three highest-severity risks are: (1) extended sales cycle depleting runway before first Build revenue, (2) solo founder burnout and key-person dependency creating a single point of failure, and (3) self-serve AI tools eroding the need for implementation consulting.** Each has a concrete mitigation plan, and none is fatal if addressed proactively.
- **Recommendation: CONDITIONAL GO** — the business is viable under all three financial scenarios (conservative through optimistic) with $40K runway, the market gap is validated, and unit economics are strong. However, launch should be contingent on completing 5 specific pre-launch validation steps within 60 days.
- **The critical decision point is Month 5.** If no Build engagement has been signed by Month 5 (the single variable that most determines Year 1 outcome), the founder must execute a pre-defined pivot or cost-reduction plan before runway enters the danger zone.

---

## Risk Matrix

The following matrix scores 17 risks across three dimensions:

- **Likelihood:** Low (<20% probability in Year 1), Medium (20-50%), High (>50%)
- **Impact:** Low (manageable with minor adjustments, <$10K revenue impact), Medium (requires significant response, $10-50K impact or 1-2 month delay), High (threatens business viability, >$50K impact or >3 month delay)
- **Severity:** Combined score — Critical (High/High), High (High/Medium or Medium/High), Medium (Medium/Medium, High/Low, Low/High), Low (Low/Low, Low/Medium, Medium/Low)

### Market Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| M1 | AI hype cools, companies pull back budgets | Low | High | Medium | Position as "AI operations" not "AI hype" — the service ladder focuses on measurable ROI ($400K/yr savings examples from Module 5). Shift messaging from "adopt AI" to "fix the AI you already bought." Companies that already spent on AI pilots need help operationalizing, not experimenting. |
| M2 | Self-serve AI tools make consulting unnecessary | Medium | High | **High** | Focus deliverables on integration, workflow design, and organizational change — the 70% of mid-market companies stuck in "pilot purgatory" (Module 1) need someone to connect tools to business processes, not install the tools themselves. Build proprietary assessment frameworks and templates that compound in value. Monitor tool capability quarterly; when tools automate a service component, shift that component to self-serve and move upmarket. |
| M3 | Economic downturn reduces discretionary AI spending | Medium | Medium | Medium | Structure pricing around ROI guarantees where possible (value-based pricing from Module 3). Position AI Brain Builds as cost-reduction tools (headcount avoidance, efficiency gains) rather than growth investments. Maintain the retainer flywheel — $6K/mo retainers are harder to cancel than $70K project decisions. Keep personal draw at minimum ($5K/mo) until 3 months of sustained revenue above $30K. |

### Competitive Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| C1 | Big consultancies move downmarket into mid-market | Medium | Medium | Medium | They structurally cannot match the cost structure — a solo founder with $1K/mo overhead vs. McKinsey's $200K+ minimum engagement (Module 2). Win on speed (4-8 week delivery vs. 6-18 month enterprise timelines) and founder-led delivery (senior architect on every engagement, not junior associates). The $15K-$100K "missing middle" is unprofitable for firms with $500/hr loaded costs. |
| C2 | Market floods with AI freelancers, race to bottom | High | Medium | **High** | Differentiate on systemic thinking — "AI Brain" positioning means interconnected workflows, not one-off chatbots (Module 2). Freelancers typically deliver point solutions; the service ladder (Diagnostic to Build to Support) creates a relationship moat. Publish case studies showing system-level ROI. Target referral-based acquisition (Module 5) where trust compounds and price sensitivity decreases. Maintain blended rates at $175-325/hr (Module 3) — never compete below $150/hr. |
| C3 | Well-funded startup productizes the "AI Brain" concept | Low | High | Medium | First-mover advantage in the advisory relationship — the startup would need to sell software, while this business sells outcomes. If a startup builds a compelling platform, become a certified implementation partner (channel strategy). The consulting relationship is the moat: clients trust the person who diagnosed their problems. Monitor AI platform startups quarterly (Relevance AI, AI21 Labs, etc.) and evaluate partnership vs. competition. |

### Delivery Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| D1 | Overcommit and underdeliver as solo founder | High | High | **Critical** | Hard cap at 2 concurrent active projects (Module 4). Maintain utilization at 60-75% — above 80% for 2 consecutive weeks triggers contractor activation. Use the capacity planning framework from Module 4: 1 Build + 2 Retainers is the maximum solo load at 40-45 hrs/week. Say no to the third Build until a contractor is vetted and available. Track weekly hours in 30-minute increments from Day 1. |
| D2 | Contractor quality issues damage client relationships | Medium | High | **High** | Vet 3-5 contractors before first client engagement (Module 4 recommends building bench in Month 3). Use paid trial tasks ($500-1,000 test projects) before assigning client work. All contractor deliverables go through founder code review before client delivery. Fixed-price contractor SOWs with milestone payments — never hourly with open scope. Maintain a "backup contractor" for every active contractor role. |
| D3 | Scope creep erodes margins | High | Medium | **High** | Every SOW includes explicit "Included" and "Excluded" sections with a change order process at defined rates (Module 3). Build in a 15-20% scope buffer on all Build estimates. Weekly client check-ins with written scope confirmation. If scope grows >10% beyond SOW, pause and re-scope as a new engagement phase. Track hours-to-estimate ratio on every project — target <110%. |

### Financial Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| F1 | Sales cycle longer than expected (8+ weeks vs. 4-6) | Medium | High | **High** | Run 8-10 free scans per month instead of 6-8 to compensate for slower conversion (Module 5 funnel math). Offer "founding client" 25% discount to accelerate first 3 deals (Module 6). Pursue parallel pipeline tracks: warm network, LinkedIn inbound, and direct outreach simultaneously. If no Diagnostic closes by Month 3, activate emergency outreach to 50+ contacts in one week. |
| F2 | Clients delay payments, cash flow crunch | Medium | Medium | Medium | Standard terms: 50% upfront on Diagnostics, 40/40/20 milestone billing on Builds, Net-15 on retainers (Module 3). For any invoice >$20K, include a 2% early-payment discount (Net-10) as incentive. Maintain $15K minimum cash reserve at all times — below this triggers immediate cost reduction. Use invoice factoring as a last resort (fees of 2-5% are acceptable in a cash emergency). |
| F3 | Underpricing early deals sets wrong expectations | Medium | Medium | Medium | Founding client pricing is explicitly framed as "25% discount for first 3 clients in exchange for case study rights and referrals" (Module 3). Every SOW states the standard rate alongside the discounted rate. After 3 founding clients, hold firm on standard pricing ($15K Diagnostic, $70K Build midpoint). If a prospect pushes back on standard pricing, reduce scope rather than reduce rate. |

### Solo Founder Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| S1 | Burnout from wearing every hat | High | High | **Critical** | Strict 45-hour weekly cap with one full day off per week — no exceptions (Module 4). Automate or eliminate low-value tasks: use AI tools for proposal drafting, reporting templates, and code generation. Outsource bookkeeping ($200/mo) and basic admin from Month 1. Schedule quarterly "reset weeks" with no client meetings. Track energy levels weekly; two consecutive "low energy" weeks triggers a workload audit and scope reduction. |
| S2 | Key-person dependency (illness halts business) | Medium | High | **High** | Build a 2-week "continuity buffer" into every client engagement: deliverables are always 1-2 weeks ahead of client expectations. Maintain documented runbooks for all client systems by Month 3 (Module 4). Secure income-replacement insurance (short-term disability, ~$150/mo). Identify one trusted senior contractor who can handle emergency client communication. For retainer clients, build self-service dashboards that reduce day-to-day dependency on the founder. |
| S3 | Revenue ceiling from capacity constraints | Medium | Medium | Medium | Plan for this from Day 1: the service ladder is designed to scale via retainers (passive-ish revenue) and contractor leverage on Builds. Target: retainer MRR covers 70%+ of break-even by Month 12 ($12K+ MRR from 2+ retainers per Module 6). First hire trigger: revenue sustained above $50K/mo for 3+ consecutive months (Module 4). Productize repeatable components (Diagnostic templates, AI pipeline libraries) to reduce founder hours per engagement by 30-40%. |

### Technical Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Severity | Specific Mitigation |
|---|------|-----------|--------|----------|-------------------|
| T1 | AI landscape shifts rapidly, tools get deprecated | High | Low | Medium | Allocate 4-5 hours/week to professional development and tool evaluation (Module 4 non-billable time). Build on abstraction layers (LangChain, LlamaIndex) rather than raw API calls to reduce vendor lock-in. Maintain working relationships with 2-3 AI platform providers (OpenAI, Anthropic, open-source models). Position the firm as "model-agnostic AI architecture" — the system design is the value, not the specific tools. |
| T2 | Client data security incident | Low | High | Medium | Implement SOC 2-aligned practices from Day 1: encrypted storage, access logging, data isolation per client (Module 4). Include data handling and liability provisions in every SOW. Carry E&O insurance ($100-200/mo, already in cost model) with cyber liability rider ($50-100/mo additional). Never store client production data on personal devices. Use isolated cloud environments per client project. Conduct self-audit quarterly. |
| T3 | AI output quality issues in production | Medium | Medium | Medium | Every Build includes defined accuracy benchmarks and evaluation criteria in the SOW (e.g., "classification accuracy >92% on test set"). Build human-in-the-loop review layers for high-stakes outputs. Include a 30-day post-launch support buffer in every Build (Module 3) specifically for quality tuning. Implement LangSmith or equivalent observability from Month 6+ (Module 6 cost model) to monitor production AI systems. Document edge cases and failure modes in client handoff materials. |

### Severity Summary

| Severity | Count | Risks |
|----------|-------|-------|
| **Critical** | 2 | D1 (Overcommit/underdeliver), S1 (Burnout) |
| **High** | 5 | M2 (Self-serve tools), C2 (Freelancer flood), D2 (Contractor quality), D3 (Scope creep), F1 (Long sales cycle) |
| **Medium** | 9 | M1, M3, C1, C3, F2, F3, S2, S3, T1, T2, T3 |
| **Low** | 0 | — |

---

## Detailed Mitigation Strategies

For each Critical and High severity risk, the following provides early warning indicators, active mitigation steps, and a contingency plan if the risk materializes.

### Critical: D1 — Overcommit and Underdeliver

**Why it's critical:** As a solo founder, there is no buffer. Taking on one project too many degrades quality on all projects simultaneously, damaging the reputation that is the business's primary asset.

**Early warning signals:**
- Weekly billable hours exceed 35 for 2 consecutive weeks
- Client deliverables slip by more than 3 business days
- Founder skips non-billable activities (free scans, content creation) for 2+ weeks
- More than 2 active Build/Diagnostic projects running simultaneously

**Active mitigation:**
1. Maintain a rolling 4-week capacity forecast updated every Monday
2. Never verbally commit to a start date without checking the capacity forecast
3. Build 20% buffer time into every project estimate (if the Build takes 6 weeks of work, schedule 7.5 weeks)
4. For each new opportunity, explicitly answer: "What am I *not* doing if I take this?"

**Contingency (if it happens):**
1. Immediately contact affected clients and reset expectations with a revised timeline — transparency preserves trust
2. Bring in an emergency contractor within 48 hours for delegable execution tasks
3. Defer all new pipeline development for 2 weeks to clear the backlog
4. Post-mortem: identify which commitment was the tipping point and adjust the capacity cap

### Critical: S1 — Burnout

**Why it's critical:** The founder *is* the business. Burnout doesn't just reduce productivity — it eliminates revenue entirely. Recovery from burnout typically takes 3-6 months, which exceeds runway.

**Early warning signals:**
- Working more than 50 hours in any single week
- Dreading client calls (emotional signal)
- Deliverable quality noticeably declining (re-work rate increasing)
- Skipping exercise, sleep, or personal commitments for 3+ consecutive weeks
- Revenue is up but satisfaction is down

**Active mitigation:**
1. Hardcode 1 full day off per week and 1 week off per quarter into the calendar *before* booking clients
2. Outsource non-core tasks immediately: bookkeeping, scheduling, basic admin
3. Build AI-powered internal tools for the repetitive parts of the job (proposal generation, report templates, code scaffolding)
4. Maintain a peer network of 3-5 other solo consultants for mutual support and accountability
5. Monthly "energy audit": score each activity (1-10) and eliminate or delegate anything scoring below 4

**Contingency (if burnout begins):**
1. Reduce client load to 1 active project immediately
2. Activate contractor bench for all delegable work on remaining projects
3. Take 1 full week off (retainer clients get self-service dashboards; active projects get a 1-week pause with client notification)
4. If recovery requires more than 2 weeks: inform retainer clients, pause new sales, and consider a 1-month sabbatical funded from cash reserves

### High: M2 — Self-Serve AI Tools Make Consulting Unnecessary

**Why it's high:** This is the structural existential risk. If ChatGPT, Copilot, or a platform like Zapier Central makes it trivially easy for mid-market companies to build their own AI workflows, the consulting model loses its foundation.

**Early warning signals:**
- Prospects saying "we're going to try it ourselves first" at increasing rates (>30% of free scans)
- No-code AI platforms raising large rounds and showing mid-market traction
- Free scan conversion rate dropping below 15% (Module 6 warning threshold)
- Average deal size declining below $10K for Diagnostics

**Active mitigation:**
1. Position on complexity, not capability — "You can build a chatbot yourself; we build the system that connects 7 departments and 4 data sources"
2. Move up the value chain: strategy, architecture, and change management are harder to automate than implementation
3. Incorporate self-serve tools *into* client deliverables — become the expert who configures and connects them, not competes with them
4. Track the AI tool landscape monthly and proactively incorporate new tools into the methodology

**Contingency:**
1. Pivot to "AI architecture advisory" — shorter, higher-margin engagements focused on design, not build
2. Develop training products (see Pivot Option #2) that help companies use self-serve tools effectively
3. Niche deeper into a vertical where domain expertise creates a moat self-serve tools cannot match

### High: C2 — Market Floods with AI Freelancers

**Why it's high:** Barriers to entry are low. Every developer with ChatGPT experience is now an "AI consultant." This compresses pricing and makes it harder to differentiate.

**Early warning signals:**
- Upwork/Toptal AI consultant listings grow >50% year-over-year
- Prospects comparing pricing to freelancer rates ($75-150/hr)
- Win rate on proposals drops below 25%
- Average time to close extends beyond 8 weeks as prospects evaluate more options

**Active mitigation:**
1. Never compete on price — compete on outcomes. Every proposal includes projected ROI, not just hourly rates
2. The service ladder (Free Scan to Diagnostic to Build to Support) is itself a differentiation — freelancers do not offer structured progression
3. Build a portfolio of case studies showing systemic results (not just "I built a chatbot")
4. Focus on referral-based acquisition (Module 5) where the recommendation short-circuits comparison shopping
5. Develop proprietary methodologies and frameworks (the "AI Brain" assessment, the "AI Readiness Scorecard") that cannot be replicated by a generic freelancer

**Contingency:**
1. Niche to a single vertical (see Pivot Option #3) where domain expertise creates an insurmountable advantage
2. Increase retainer percentage of revenue — retainer clients are less likely to switch to a freelancer mid-relationship
3. Partner with 2-3 high-quality freelancers as subcontractors rather than competing with them

### High: D2 — Contractor Quality Issues

**Why it's high:** Client trust is with the founder. If a contractor delivers poor work under the founder's brand, the damage is asymmetric — it takes months to build trust and one bad deliverable to destroy it.

**Early warning signals:**
- Contractor deliverables requiring >30% rework in code review
- Client feedback mentioning quality inconsistencies
- Contractor missing deadlines by more than 2 business days
- Contractor communication gaps (unresponsive for >24 hours during active projects)

**Active mitigation:**
1. Pre-engagement vetting: paid trial project ($500-1,000) before any client work assignment
2. Every contractor deliverable goes through founder review before client delivery — this is non-negotiable
3. Fixed-price SOWs with milestone payments (not hourly) — aligns incentives and caps exposure
4. Maintain a bench of 3-5 vetted contractors across key skill areas (frontend, backend, data engineering)
5. Weekly check-ins with contractors during active projects; daily standups during critical phases

**Contingency:**
1. Remove underperforming contractor immediately — do not attempt to coach during an active client project
2. Absorb the remaining work personally (this is why the 2-project cap matters)
3. Proactively communicate with the client: "We're bringing in our senior architect (the founder) to ensure quality on this deliverable"
4. Post-mortem: update vetting criteria and trial project requirements

### High: D3 — Scope Creep Erodes Margins

**Why it's high:** Scope creep is the silent margin killer in consulting. At $70K Build projects with 50-65% target margin (Module 3), a 25% scope overrun turns a $35K profit into a $17K profit — cutting income nearly in half.

**Early warning signals:**
- Hours tracked exceed estimate by >10% at the project midpoint
- Client requesting "small additions" more than twice per week
- SOW "Excluded" items starting to appear in client emails as expectations
- Founder spending more than 20% of project hours on unplanned tasks

**Active mitigation:**
1. SOW includes explicit "Included/Excluded" lists and a numbered change order process
2. Change orders priced at 1.2x standard rate (premium for disruption) with written client approval before work begins
3. Weekly scope check: compare actual work against SOW line items; flag any deviation immediately
4. Build 15-20% scope buffer into every estimate (quote 7 weeks for 6 weeks of work)
5. At the 50% project milestone, conduct a formal scope review with the client and re-confirm remaining deliverables

**Contingency:**
1. If scope has already crept: have an honest conversation with the client, document the additions, and present a change order for the overage
2. For the current project, absorb reasonable overages to preserve the relationship; apply learnings to the next SOW
3. If a client pattern emerges (chronic scope creep), adjust the client's next engagement to value-based pricing with defined outcomes, not defined tasks

### High: F1 — Sales Cycle Longer Than Expected

**Why it's high:** The financial model assumes first Diagnostic revenue by Month 2-3 (base/conservative). If the first deal closes at Month 5 instead of Month 3, cash on hand drops from $29,200 to approximately $18,000 under conservative assumptions with $40K runway — still viable but uncomfortably thin.

**Early warning signals:**
- 0 Diagnostics closed by end of Month 3
- Free scan conversion rate below 15% after 10+ scans completed
- Prospects requesting "more time to think" or "need internal approval" at increasing rates
- Pipeline weighted value below $30K at Month 3

**Active mitigation:**
1. Begin free scan outreach in the pre-launch period (Month -1 to Month 0) to prime the pipeline
2. Run 8-10 free scans/month instead of 6-8 to compensate for lower conversion rates
3. Pursue at least 2 warm network leads in parallel with inbound from Day 1
4. Offer founding client pricing (25% discount) with urgency: "3 founding client slots available, 1 already filled"
5. If no Diagnostic by Month 3: activate a "fire sale" — offer a $5,000 mini-Diagnostic (2-day intensive) to get a client on the books and generate a case study

**Contingency:**
1. At Month 4 with no revenue: cut personal draw to $3,500/mo (below minimum, but buys 2 additional months)
2. Pursue a fractional/part-time consulting role (2 days/week) to generate $6-8K/mo while continuing to build the pipeline
3. If Month 5 with no revenue: trigger the hard go/no-go decision (see Decision Timeline)

---

## Go/No-Go Decision Framework

### Green Lights — Launch

All of the following must be true before committing full-time to the business. These can be validated during a 60-day pre-launch validation period while still employed or during the first 30 days.

| # | Criterion | Measurement | Threshold |
|---|-----------|-------------|-----------|
| G1 | Free scan demand validated | Completed free AI Opportunity Scans | 5+ scans completed with 2+ (40%+) expressing interest in paid Diagnostic |
| G2 | Beachhead willingness-to-pay confirmed | Direct pricing conversations during scans | At least 2 prospects have seen pricing ($10-20K Diagnostic range) without rejecting it; 1+ has said "send me a proposal" |
| G3 | At least 1 signed engagement or LOI | Signed SOW, LOI, or paid deposit | 1+ signed document or payment received (even a $5K mini-Diagnostic counts) |
| G4 | Unit economics confirmed | Margin analysis on first 1-2 engagements (or detailed estimates) | Gross margin above 55% on Diagnostics (solo-delivered) and above 45% on projected Builds (with contractor costs) |
| G5 | Positioning resonates | Qualitative feedback from 10+ conversations | 7+ out of 10 prospects can articulate what makes the "AI Brain" offering different when asked; 0 prospects confuse the offering with generic "AI chatbot" services |
| G6 | Financial runway confirmed | Bank balance | $30,000+ liquid cash available at launch date (per Module 6 minimum recommendation) |
| G7 | Legal/operational foundation set | LLC, insurance, contracts | LLC formed, E&O insurance active, SOW template reviewed by attorney |

**Decision: If all 7 green lights are met, proceed with full launch.**

### Yellow Lights — Proceed with Caution

Any of the following trigger a "conditional launch" with modified parameters:

| # | Condition | Implication | Modified Approach |
|---|-----------|-------------|-------------------|
| Y1 | Only 3-4 free scans completed (not 5+) and 1 shows paid interest | Pipeline is thinner than ideal | Launch, but maintain a part-time income source (fractional consulting, contract work) for the first 3 months while building pipeline. Increase free scan volume to 10+/month. |
| Y2 | No signed engagement yet, but 2+ proposals are outstanding | Sales cycle is slower than modeled | Launch, but use the conservative financial scenario as the operating plan. Cut personal draw to $4,000/mo for Months 1-3. Set a hard deadline: if no signed deal by Month 3, trigger Yellow-to-Red reassessment. |
| Y3 | Runway is $20-30K (below the $30K recommended minimum) | Cash buffer is thin under conservative scenario (lowest point: $9,200 with $20K) | Launch only with at least 1 signed engagement generating revenue in Month 1-2. Without a signed deal, do not launch — the conservative scenario with $20K runway has a dangerously low $9,200 cash floor (Module 6). |
| Y4 | Pricing conversations suggest the market expects lower rates (Diagnostic at $5-8K vs. $10-20K) | Market may be more price-sensitive than modeled | Launch, but adjust to SMB pricing tier initially ($5-10K Diagnostics, $25-50K Builds) and validate whether mid-market pricing works with different positioning or a different beachhead segment. |
| Y5 | Only 1 beachhead segment shows traction (e.g., legal firms respond but accounting firms don't) | Narrower addressable market than assumed | Launch focused exclusively on the responding segment. This is not necessarily bad — niche focus can accelerate reputation building. Revisit broader market after 3-5 clients in the niche. |

**Decision: If 1-2 yellow lights are present with no red lights, launch is acceptable with the specified modifications. If 3+ yellow lights are present, treat as a red light.**

### Red Lights — Pause or Pivot

Any single red light means "do not launch full-time" without first resolving the issue:

| # | Condition | Implication | Required Action |
|---|-----------|-------------|-----------------|
| R1 | Fewer than 3 free scans completed in 60 days of active outreach | Demand signal is absent or outreach is fundamentally broken | Do not launch. Diagnose: Is the target segment wrong? Is the offer unclear? Is the distribution channel broken? Conduct 20+ additional conversations before re-evaluating. |
| R2 | 0 out of 5+ prospects express willingness to pay for a Diagnostic | The service is not solving a problem prospects will pay to fix | Do not launch. Pivot the offer: consider training/workshops instead of consulting, or target a different segment. The "AI Brain" concept may need repositioning. |
| R3 | Every pricing conversation results in rejection at $10K+ Diagnostic level | Pricing is fundamentally misaligned with market | Do not launch at current pricing. Test at $5K for a "mini-Diagnostic" (1-week sprint). If $5K also rejected, the problem is demand, not price — return to R2. |
| R4 | Runway below $20K with no signed engagement | Risk of running out of cash before first revenue under any scenario | Do not launch full-time. Pursue hybrid model: maintain part-time income while running 3-5 free scans per month as a side activity. Re-evaluate when either (a) a signed deal materializes or (b) runway reaches $30K+. |
| R5 | Founder has unresolved personal commitments that prevent 40+ hrs/week for 6 months | The business requires full-time intensity in Year 1 | Do not launch until commitments are resolved. A half-effort launch is worse than no launch — it burns runway without generating sufficient pipeline. |
| R6 | After 30 days of conversations, the competitive landscape has a direct competitor offering the same service at lower price in the same beachhead segment | Timing or positioning is wrong | Do not launch into that segment. Either (a) identify a different beachhead where the competitor is absent, or (b) develop a clear differentiation that prospects articulate back to you unprompted. |

---

## Decision Timeline

Based on the financial model's $40K runway assumption, personal draw schedule, and the three revenue scenarios:

### Pre-Launch: Day -60 to Day 0

**Objective:** Validate green/yellow/red light criteria while still earning income or before burning runway.

| Week | Activity | Success Signal |
|------|----------|----------------|
| Week 1-2 | Set up LLC, insurance, basic tooling, SOW templates | Legal and operational foundation complete |
| Week 2-4 | Conduct 5-8 free AI Opportunity Scans via warm network | 5+ scans completed |
| Week 3-6 | Hold pricing conversations; send 2+ proposals | 2+ proposals outstanding or 1+ signed engagement |
| Week 4-8 | Evaluate green/yellow/red light criteria | Go/No-Go decision made |

**Decision point: Day 0 (launch date)**
- All green lights met: Full launch
- Yellow lights present: Conditional launch with modifications
- Red lights present: Do not launch; address specific issues

### Month 3: First Checkpoint

**What's evaluated:** Pipeline health and first revenue.

| Metric | Green | Yellow | Red |
|--------|-------|--------|-----|
| Free scans completed | 12+ cumulative | 8-11 | <8 |
| Diagnostics closed | 1+ | 0, but 2+ proposals pending | 0 with no active proposals |
| Revenue recognized | $10,000+ | $0 but signed deal pending | $0 with no pipeline |
| Cash on hand | >$25,000 | $18,000-$25,000 | <$18,000 |
| Scan-to-Diagnostic conversion | >20% | 10-20% | <10% |

**Actions:**
- All green: Continue executing base case plan. Increase scan volume to 6-8/month.
- Any yellow: Increase outreach intensity. Activate all network contacts. Consider founding client pricing if not already offered.
- Any red: Cut personal draw to $3,500/mo. Evaluate pivot to hybrid model (part-time income + part-time consulting build). Begin exploring Pivot Options.

### Month 5: Second Checkpoint (Critical)

**What's evaluated:** Whether the Build engine has started. This is the most important checkpoint because Build revenue ($40-100K) is the primary revenue driver comprising 55-59% of Year 1 revenue across all scenarios (Module 6).

| Metric | Green | Yellow | Red |
|--------|-------|--------|-----|
| Diagnostics closed | 2+ | 1 (with Build pipeline) | 0-1 (no Build pipeline) |
| Build engagements signed | 1+ | 0, but 1+ Diagnostic converting to Build proposal | 0 with no Build pipeline |
| Revenue recognized (cumulative) | $40,000+ | $15,000-$40,000 | <$15,000 |
| Cash on hand | >$30,000 | $20,000-$30,000 | <$20,000 |
| MRR trajectory | On track for $6K by Month 6 | Retainer conversations happening | No retainer prospects |

**Actions:**
- All green: On track for base case or better. Begin contractor vetting for Build support.
- Any yellow: This is the "last chance" to course-correct. Double free scan volume. Offer deep founding-client discounts. Pursue a quick-hit $5K mini-Diagnostic to generate cash and case study.
- Any red: **Hard decision required.** Evaluate: (a) pivot to hybrid model immediately, (b) execute Pivot Option #3 (niche to single vertical) for last 7 months, or (c) begin Pivot Option #4 (join existing firm) exploration while continuing pipeline at reduced intensity.

### Month 8: Third Checkpoint

**What's evaluated:** Retainer flywheel and sustainable revenue.

| Metric | Green | Yellow | Red |
|--------|-------|--------|-----|
| Active retainers | 1+ | 0, but Build completing with retainer conversation | 0 with no Build-to-retainer pipeline |
| Monthly revenue | $25,000+ | $15,000-$25,000 | <$15,000 |
| MRR | $6,000+ | $0 but retainer proposal pending | $0 with no prospect |
| Cumulative revenue | $120,000+ | $75,000-$120,000 | <$75,000 |
| Cash on hand | >$50,000 | $30,000-$50,000 | <$30,000 |

**Actions:**
- All green: On track. Begin planning for first hire (Month 12-18 target per Module 4).
- Any yellow: Focus 100% on converting existing clients to retainers. Pause new free scans temporarily to deliver exceptional results on current clients.
- Any red: Execute pivot plan. At this point, the business has had 8 months to validate and has not reached sustainable revenue. The remaining 4 months of runway (at minimum draw) provide time for an orderly transition to a pivot option.

### Month 10: Runway Danger Zone Assessment

**What's evaluated:** Is Year 2 viable?

| Metric | Green | Yellow | Red |
|--------|-------|--------|-----|
| MRR | $12,000+ (2 retainers) | $6,000-$12,000 | <$6,000 |
| Cash on hand | >$80,000 | $40,000-$80,000 | <$40,000 |
| Pipeline for next 90 days | $100K+ weighted pipeline | $50-100K weighted pipeline | <$50K pipeline |
| Monthly revenue trend | Growing or stable | Flat | Declining |

**Actions:**
- All green: Plan for Year 2 growth. Set hiring timeline. Consider office space.
- Any yellow: Year 2 is viable but growth will be slower than planned. Maintain solo model. Focus on retainer acquisition.
- Any red: Year 2 as a full-time solo business is not viable at current trajectory. Begin transitioning to the highest-feasibility pivot option within 60 days.

---

## Pivot Options

Ranked by feasibility for a solo technical founder with deep AI/ML + SWE skills, assuming the consulting model has not achieved target traction by Month 8-10.

### Pivot Option #1: Niche to Single Vertical (Feasibility: 9/10)

**What it means:** Abandon the broad "mid-market professional services" beachhead and focus exclusively on one sub-vertical (e.g., only law firms, only accounting firms, or only mid-market financial services).

**Why highest feasibility:**
- Requires zero new skills or infrastructure — same services, narrower target
- The consulting model is *already working* at some level; the problem is likely too-broad positioning, not wrong model
- Vertical specialization accelerates referrals (law firms talk to other law firms)
- Enables "best in class" positioning: "the AI consultant for law firms" vs. "an AI consultant"
- Can be executed in 2 weeks (update positioning, website, outreach targets)

**When to execute:** If Month 5 checkpoint shows traction with one segment but not others, or if conversion rates are low due to positioning confusion.

**Risk:** The chosen vertical may be too small or too resistant to AI adoption.

### Pivot Option #2: Focus on Training/Education (Feasibility: 7/10)

**What it means:** Shift from implementation consulting to AI training workshops, courses, and enablement programs for mid-market companies.

**Why high feasibility:**
- Leverages the same expertise and relationships built during free scans and Diagnostics
- Shorter sales cycle: training purchases are lower-commitment ($2-10K per workshop vs. $70K Build)
- Scalable: one workshop can serve 10-20 participants; online courses can serve unlimited
- Can be launched within 30 days using existing content from Diagnostics and free scan materials
- Compatible with a hybrid model (part-time training revenue + part-time consulting pipeline)

**Revenue model:** $3-5K for half-day workshops, $5-10K for full-day intensives, $500-2K for online courses. Target: 3-4 workshops/month = $12-20K/month revenue at 80%+ margin.

**When to execute:** If free scans generate interest in "learning about AI" but not in "hiring someone to build AI," training is the product the market is asking for.

**Risk:** Training market is competitive and commoditizing rapidly. Margins are lower per hour than consulting.

### Pivot Option #3: Productize into SaaS (Feasibility: 5/10)

**What it means:** Take the "AI Brain" framework — the assessment methodology, the pipeline templates, the integration patterns — and build it into a self-serve software product that mid-market companies can use without a consultant.

**Why moderate feasibility:**
- The founder has deep SWE skills to build the product
- Consulting engagements provide direct user research for product requirements
- Recurring SaaS revenue ($500-5K/mo per customer) is more scalable than consulting
- Could be launched as an MVP within 3-4 months of focused development

**Why not higher:**
- Requires significant upfront development time with no revenue during build phase
- SaaS sales cycle for mid-market is 3-6 months (Module 5 sales cycle data)
- Competes with well-funded AI platform startups (Relevance AI, etc.)
- Consulting and SaaS are very different businesses — attempting both splits focus
- Requires marketing, support, and product development skills beyond consulting

**When to execute:** If multiple clients ask "can we just use your framework ourselves?" — that is the market signal for productization.

**Risk:** High burn rate during development phase. Product-market fit is not guaranteed. Requires different go-to-market.

### Pivot Option #4: Join Existing AI Firm (Feasibility: 8/10)

**What it means:** Abandon the solo model and join an existing AI consultancy or boutique firm as a senior consultant, principal, or partner — bringing the methodology, client relationships, and pipeline built during the solo attempt.

**Why high feasibility:**
- Immediate income stability — eliminates runway risk
- The consulting experience, case studies, and methodology developed during the solo attempt are highly valuable to a firm
- Mid-market AI boutiques are actively hiring experienced consultants (Module 2: boutique firms growing into this space)
- Can be positioned as a strategic choice rather than a failure: "I validated the market, now I'm joining a platform to scale"
- Can be executed within 30-60 days

**When to execute:** If Month 8 checkpoint shows red lights and the founder assesses that the problem is scale/sales capacity rather than market demand. Also appropriate if the founder discovers they prefer delivery over business development.

**Risk:** Loss of independence and equity upside. Compensation is likely $150-250K rather than the $275-716K Year 1 revenue range of the solo model.

### Pivot Option #5: Open-Source Framework + Monetize (Feasibility: 4/10)

**What it means:** Open-source the "AI Brain" assessment framework, pipeline templates, and integration components. Monetize through paid support, hosted cloud versions, enterprise features, and consulting on top of the framework.

**Why lower feasibility:**
- Open-source community building takes 6-12+ months to reach meaningful adoption
- Monetization of open-source is notoriously difficult (the "1% rule" — only 1% of users pay)
- Requires significant investment in documentation, community management, and DevRel
- Does not generate revenue in the short term — exacerbates the runway problem
- The founder's advantage is business context + AI expertise, not a novel technology

**When to execute:** Only if there is genuine technical innovation worth open-sourcing (e.g., a novel AI pipeline framework that solves a problem existing tools do not). This is a long-term play, not a pivot from a failing consulting business.

**Risk:** Dilutes the competitive moat (anyone can use the framework). Community building is a full-time job. Monetization timeline is 12-24+ months.

### Pivot Option Comparison

| Option | Feasibility | Time to Revenue | Revenue Potential (Year 1) | Runway Required | Best For |
|--------|-------------|-----------------|---------------------------|-----------------|----------|
| #1: Niche vertical | 9/10 | 2-4 weeks | Same as current ($275-716K) | Same | Positioning problem, not model problem |
| #2: Training | 7/10 | 30-60 days | $120-240K | $10-15K | Demand for education, not implementation |
| #3: SaaS | 5/10 | 4-6 months | $0-50K (Year 1), $200K+ (Year 2) | $50K+ | "Can I do this myself?" market signal |
| #4: Join a firm | 8/10 | 30-60 days | $150-250K (salary) | $0 | Founder prefers delivery over sales |
| #5: Open-source | 4/10 | 12-24 months | $0-30K | $80K+ | Genuine technical innovation exists |

---

## Overall Recommendation

### Verdict: CONDITIONAL GO

The business should launch, subject to completing the 7 green-light criteria within a 60-day pre-launch validation period.

### Reasoning

**The case for GO:**

1. **Market demand is validated.** The $6.4B AI consulting market growing at 24-27% CAGR (Module 1) with a confirmed "missing middle" gap at $15-100K (Module 2) provides a clear market opportunity. The 50% of mid-market companies stuck in "pilot purgatory" need exactly what this business offers.

2. **Unit economics are strong.** Gross margins of 70-85% on solo-delivered services and 50-65% on contractor-augmented Builds (Module 3) are exceptional. A single mid-market client generates $73-147K in 12-month LTV. The business needs only 3-5 active clients to be highly profitable.

3. **The financial model works under all scenarios.** Conservative: $275K Year 1 revenue, break-even Month 5. Base: $397K, break-even Month 3. Optimistic: $716K, break-even Month 1 (Module 6). Even the conservative case generates $143K in net cash by Month 12.

4. **Operational overhead is minimal.** At $1,000/mo in growth-phase fixed costs (Module 4), the business has a low burn rate that extends runway significantly. Monthly break-even (including $5K personal draw) is only $5,400 in the early months.

5. **The service ladder creates a natural flywheel.** Free Scan to Diagnostic to Build to Retainer (Module 3) generates compounding MRR that de-risks the business over time. By Month 12, the base case has $18K MRR from 3 retainers — retainer revenue alone exceeds break-even.

**The case for CONDITIONAL (not unconditional) GO:**

1. **Two risks are rated Critical.** Solo founder overcommitment (D1) and burnout (S1) are inherent structural risks that cannot be fully mitigated — only managed. The business has zero redundancy.

2. **Pre-launch validation is essential.** The financial model assumes first Diagnostic by Month 2-3, but this is only possible if pipeline-building starts before Day 1. Without pre-launch validation (5+ free scans, 1+ signed engagement), the model's assumptions are untested.

3. **The $40K runway is adequate but not generous.** Under the conservative scenario, the lowest cash point is $29,200 (Month 2) — a comfortable margin. But with $20K runway, the lowest point drops to $9,200, which is dangerously close to zero after accounting for unexpected expenses.

4. **The competitive landscape is intensifying.** AI freelancers are flooding the market (C2, rated High severity) and self-serve tools are improving rapidly (M2, rated High severity). The window for establishing a consulting practice with defensible positioning is 12-24 months, not indefinite.

### What Must Be True for Success

The following 5 conditions are necessary (not merely helpful) for Year 1 viability:

1. **First Build engagement signed by Month 5.** Build revenue is 55-59% of Year 1 revenue across all scenarios. Without at least one Build in the first 5 months, the retainer flywheel never starts, and the business remains dependent on one-time Diagnostic revenue that does not compound.

2. **Free scan pipeline produces 6+ scans/month by Month 3.** At 25-35% conversion (Module 5), 6 scans/month yields 1.5-2 Diagnostics/month — the minimum to keep the funnel moving. Below this volume, there is not enough pipeline to convert.

3. **Blended gross margins stay above 50%.** If margins fall below 50% due to scope creep, underpricing, or contractor cost overruns, the business cannot sustain the founder's personal draw while covering costs. This is the financial kill line.

4. **The founder maintains 60-75% utilization without exceeding 45 hours/week.** Below 60% means not enough revenue. Above 75% means no time for pipeline development. Above 45 hours/week means burnout is approaching. All three boundaries must hold simultaneously.

5. **At least 1 retainer client active by Month 8.** The retainer flywheel ($6K/mo per retainer) is what transforms the business from "feast or famine" project revenue to a sustainable operation. Without retainers, Year 2 requires the same level of constant new-client acquisition as Year 1 — which is not sustainable for a solo founder.

### Single Biggest Risk and How to Manage It

**The single biggest risk is the interaction between "time-to-first-Build" (F1) and "solo founder burnout" (S1).**

Here is why these interact dangerously: if the first Build takes longer to close than expected (F1), the founder compensates by increasing outreach intensity and accepting suboptimal engagements. This increases hours worked and cognitive load. The pressure of declining runway adds stress. By the time the first Build arrives, the founder may be operating at 80%+ utilization with depleted energy — exactly when they need to deliver their best work on the highest-value engagement. Poor Build delivery damages the client relationship, which kills the retainer conversion, which eliminates the flywheel that makes the business sustainable.

**How to manage it:**

1. **Pre-launch pipeline development.** Conduct 5-8 free scans before Day 1 so the sales cycle clock starts before the runway clock.
2. **Financial buffer.** Start with $40K+ runway (not $30K) to provide 7+ months of minimum burn without revenue, removing the pressure to close deals prematurely.
3. **Emotional circuit breakers.** If weekly hours exceed 50 or stress levels are high for 2 consecutive weeks, cancel all non-essential meetings and take 2 days off. This is counterintuitive when runway is burning, but a burned-out founder closes zero deals.
4. **Month 5 hard checkpoint.** If no Build is signed by Month 5, execute a pre-defined plan (hybrid income, cost reduction, or pivot) rather than grinding harder. The plan should be written and committed to *before* launch — deciding in a moment of stress leads to poor decisions.
5. **Monthly founder health check.** Every month, answer honestly: "Am I excited to do this work today?" If the answer is "no" for 3 consecutive months, that is a signal to explore Pivot Option #4 (join an existing firm) regardless of financial metrics.

# Module 8: SMB & Local Business Expansion

## Executive Summary

- **The U.S. has 33.2 million small businesses, the vast majority with fewer than 20 employees, and AI adoption among them remains below 15% — creating a massive underserved market for custom AI automation that is structurally ignored by enterprise consultancies and too bespoke for off-the-shelf SaaS tools.** Service-based local businesses (plumbers, HVAC, salons, restaurants, cleaning services, contractors, accountants, dentists) are the sweet spot: they have painful manual workflows, enough revenue to pay $1-5K for automation, and near-zero in-house technical capability.
- **The SMB lane operates as an equal, parallel business alongside the mid-market lane — not a downmarket compromise.** The same core methodology applies (discover operations, identify bottlenecks, build custom AI automation), but right-sized: $500-5,000 per engagement, delivered in days to weeks, with $200-500/mo support retainers. At a base case of 4-6 SMB clients per month averaging $2,500 each, the lane generates $10,000-15,000/mo in additional revenue with 55-65% gross margins.
- **The two-lane strategy creates a more resilient business than either lane alone.** SMB provides steady cash flow, high volume, and pattern recognition that accelerates delivery speed; mid-market provides large revenue events and marquee case studies. By Month 12-18, the SMB lane is a candidate for delegation to a junior hire or trained contractor, freeing the founder to focus on mid-market while maintaining the revenue stream.

---

## SMB Market Opportunity

### Market Size

The U.S. small business landscape is enormous and overwhelmingly composed of very small firms:

| Metric | Figure | Source Context |
|---|---|---|
| Total small businesses in the U.S. | 33.2 million | SBA Office of Advocacy, 2024 |
| Businesses with fewer than 20 employees | 31.4 million (89.0% of all employer firms) | U.S. Census Bureau, County Business Patterns |
| Businesses with fewer than 500 employees | 99.9% of all U.S. businesses | SBA FAQ, 2024 |
| Nonemployer firms (sole proprietors, no employees) | 28.0 million | Census Bureau Nonemployer Statistics |
| Small businesses with 1-19 employees | 5.4 million | Census Bureau, Statistics of U.S. Businesses |
| Small businesses with 20-99 employees | 680,000 | Census Bureau, Statistics of U.S. Businesses |
| Small businesses with 100-499 employees | 92,000 | Census Bureau, Statistics of U.S. Businesses |
| Total small business revenue | ~$16.8 trillion annually | SBA estimates |
| Average revenue per small employer firm | ~$1.2 million | Calculated from Census data |

**Target segment for the SMB lane:** Employer firms with 1-50 employees generating $200K-$5M in annual revenue. This captures roughly 5-6 million businesses — service-heavy, operationally manual, and large enough to afford $1-5K automation projects.

### AI Adoption Among Small Businesses

AI adoption among small businesses is very low, but awareness is rising quickly:

| Metric | Figure | Source Context |
|---|---|---|
| Small businesses currently using AI (any form) | 10-15% | U.S. Chamber of Commerce Small Business Index, 2025; SCORE survey data |
| Small businesses "interested in" or "considering" AI | 40-50% | U.S. Chamber of Commerce Technology Adoption Survey, 2025 |
| Small businesses that have tried AI tools and stopped | 15-20% | SCORE Small Business AI Survey, 2025 |
| Top barrier to AI adoption | "Don't know where to start" (48%) | U.S. Chamber / MetLife Small Business Index |
| Second barrier | "Too expensive" (31%) | Same |
| Third barrier | "Don't trust AI / worried about accuracy" (27%) | Same |
| Small businesses spending $0 on technology consulting | ~75% | Alignable / Clutch SMB surveys |

**Key insight:** The primary barrier is not cost or resistance — it is a knowledge gap. Small business owners do not know what AI can do for their specific operations, cannot evaluate solutions, and have no trusted advisor to guide them. This is exactly the gap the discovery-first, custom-build model fills.

### Target Business Types and Pain Points

The SMB lane targets service-based and local businesses where manual operations create direct, quantifiable inefficiency. The following matrix maps business types to their highest-impact automation opportunities:

### Why This Market Is Underserved

The SMB AI automation market sits in a structural gap:

| Current Provider | Why They Do Not Serve Local SMBs Well |
|---|---|
| **Enterprise AI consultancies** (Deloitte, Accenture, McKinsey) | Minimum engagement $200K+. Cannot economically serve a plumber with $1M revenue. Their operating model requires large teams and long timelines. |
| **Boutique AI firms** (10-50 person shops) | Minimum engagement $25K-$75K. Their cost structure (office, employees, benefits) cannot profitably deliver $2K projects. |
| **Freelance AI engineers** (Upwork, Toptal) | Technically capable but lack business context. A freelancer can build a chatbot but cannot diagnose that the plumber's real problem is dispatch scheduling, not a chatbot. No discovery process, no operational understanding. |
| **SaaS platforms** (Jobber, ServiceTitan, Toast) | Offer generic, one-size-fits-all features. A plumber gets the same scheduling module as every other plumber. No customization for their specific workflow, integrations, or customer communication style. |
| **No-code/low-code tools** (Zapier, Make, n8n) | Powerful but require the business owner to understand what to automate and how. The knowledge gap is the barrier, not the tool. Most small business owners do not have 20 hours to learn Zapier, and the automations they would build are basic compared to what an expert configures. |
| **"AI agencies" (emerging)** | Mostly template-and-deploy models: pre-built chatbots, generic email sequences. Not genuinely custom. Often oversell and underdeliver. Create market cynicism. |

**The gap this business fills:** A trusted advisor who understands the local business's actual operations, identifies the highest-impact automation opportunities, and builds genuinely custom solutions using AI tools — at a price point ($500-$5,000) that makes economic sense for a business generating $300K-$5M in revenue.

## Service Model for SMB

### The SMB Service Ladder

The SMB service model mirrors the mid-market approach (discover, diagnose, build, support) but is compressed in scope, timeline, and price. The methodology is the same; the scale is different.

#### Step 1: Discovery Call (Free or $99-199)

**What it is:** A 30-60 minute conversation where the founder (or trained junior) walks through the business owner's daily operations, identifies manual workflows, and maps the top 3 automation opportunities.

**Deliverable:** A 1-page "Automation Opportunity Brief" — three specific automation recommendations ranked by impact and ease of implementation, with rough cost estimates.

**Timeline:** Scheduled within 3-5 business days of inquiry. Brief delivered within 24-48 hours of call.

**Pricing options:**
- **Free** — preferred for lead generation when building initial SMB client base. Cost to deliver: 1-1.5 hours of time.
- **$99-199** — low-friction paid option that filters out tire-kickers and establishes value from the first interaction. Consider this after the first 10-15 free calls have validated the conversion rate.

**Strategic purpose:** Same as the mid-market Free Scan but compressed. The brief surfaces problems the business owner knows they have but did not realize could be solved with automation. It creates the "oh, you can do that?" moment that drives the sale.

#### Step 2: Custom Build

**What it is:** Design and implementation of a custom AI automation tailored to the specific business's operations, tools, and workflows. This is not a template deployed identically to every plumber — it is a system configured and integrated for this particular business's scheduling software, customer communication style, service area, and operational quirks.

**What "custom" means at this scale:**
- Using the same underlying tools (LLM APIs, automation platforms like n8n/Make/Zapier, voice AI, SMS/email APIs) but configured specifically for this business
- Integrating with the business's existing tools (their specific CRM, scheduling software, accounting system, phone system)
- Tuned to their terminology, service offerings, geographic area, and customer communication tone
- Workflow logic that reflects how they actually operate, not a generic template

**Pricing tiers:**

| Tier | Price Range | Scope | Typical Delivery | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Simple Automation** | $500-$1,500 | Single workflow, 1-2 integrations | 1-3 days | Automated review request sequences, appointment reminder system, basic lead follow-up, invoice generation from work orders |
| **Standard Automation** | $1,500-$3,000 | Multi-step workflow, 2-4 integrations | 3-7 days | AI scheduling + dispatch optimization, customer intake + document processing, inventory tracking + auto-reorder, multi-channel follow-up system |
| **Complex Automation** | $3,000-$5,000 | Multiple interconnected workflows, 4+ integrations, AI reasoning | 1-3 weeks | Full customer lifecycle automation (lead to invoice), AI-assisted estimating from photos/descriptions, multi-location scheduling + routing + customer communication, integrated bookkeeping + client management system |

**Payment terms:** 50% upfront, 50% on delivery. For projects over $3,000: 50% upfront, 25% at midpoint, 25% on delivery.

#### Step 3: Support & Maintenance

**What it is:** Ongoing monitoring, optimization, and adjustment of deployed automations. Includes fixing issues, adjusting workflows as the business evolves, and minor enhancements.

**Pricing:**

| Tier | Monthly Price | Includes | Best For |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Basic** | $200/mo | Monitoring, bug fixes, 1 hr/mo of adjustments, email support | Simple automations that rarely change |
| **Standard** | $350/mo | Everything in Basic + 2 hrs/mo of enhancements, priority support, monthly check-in call | Businesses with evolving needs |
| **Premium** | $500/mo | Everything in Standard + 4 hrs/mo of enhancements, same-day support, quarterly strategy call | Complex automations, multiple workflows |

**Minimum commitment:** 3 months. Month-to-month after initial term.

**Support economics note:** SMB support retainers are high-margin if scoped correctly. At $350/mo with 1.5 hours of actual work per month (most months), the effective rate is $233/hr. The key is not over-servicing — automations built correctly should require minimal ongoing attention.

### How SMB Differs from Mid-Market (Side-by-Side)

| Dimension | Mid-Market Lane | SMB/Local Lane |
|---|---|---|
| **Discovery** | Free 60-min scan + 2-4 week Diagnostic ($10-25K) | Free or $99-199 discovery call (30-60 min) |
| **Build scope** | Multi-system, cross-department, 4-12 weeks | Single or multi-workflow, 1-3 weeks |
| **Build price** | $40,000-$100,000 | $500-$5,000 |
| **Support retainer** | $4,000-$8,000/mo | $200-$500/mo |
| **Decision maker** | VP/Director, needs internal buy-in | Owner, decides on the spot |
| **Sales cycle** | 4-8 weeks | 1-7 days |
| **Technical complexity** | RAG systems, multi-agent workflows, enterprise integrations | Workflow automation, API integrations, AI-assisted communication |
| **Client technical sophistication** | Has IT team or technical staff | Minimal — "I just need it to work" |
| **Primary value proposition** | "Build the AI brain of your company" | "Save you 10+ hours/week and never miss another customer" |
| **Relationship duration** | 12-24+ months | 3-12 months (may expand) |

## Unit Economics

### Per-Project Economics

#### Simple Automation ($500-$1,500)

| Metric | Low End | Midpoint | High End |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $500 | $1,000 | $1,500 |
| Founder hours | 3 hrs | 5 hrs | 8 hrs |
| Founder cost (@$150/hr opportunity cost) | $450 | $750 | $1,200 |
| API/tool costs | $5 | $15 | $30 |
| **Total COGS** | **$455** | **$765** | **$1,230** |
| **Gross profit** | **$45** | **$235** | **$270** |
| **Gross margin** | **9%** | **24%** | **18%** |
| Effective hourly rate | $167 | $200 | $188 |

**Assessment:** Simple automations at $500 are barely profitable at the founder's opportunity cost. The minimum viable price for founder-delivered simple automations is $750-$1,000. Below that, they only make sense if (a) the founder is filling idle capacity between mid-market projects or (b) a junior hire or contractor delivers them at a lower hourly cost.

#### Standard Automation ($1,500-$3,000)

| Metric | Low End | Midpoint | High End |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $1,500 | $2,250 | $3,000 |
| Founder hours | 8 hrs | 12 hrs | 16 hrs |
| Founder cost (@$150/hr) | $1,200 | $1,800 | $2,400 |
| API/tool costs | $20 | $40 | $75 |
| **Total COGS** | **$1,220** | **$1,840** | **$2,475** |
| **Gross profit** | **$280** | **$410** | **$525** |
| **Gross margin** | **19%** | **18%** | **18%** |
| Effective hourly rate | $188 | $188 | $188 |

#### Complex Automation ($3,000-$5,000)

| Metric | Low End | Midpoint | High End |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $3,000 | $4,000 | $5,000 |
| Founder hours | 16 hrs | 22 hrs | 30 hrs |
| Founder cost (@$150/hr) | $2,400 | $3,300 | $4,500 |
| API/tool costs | $50 | $100 | $200 |
| **Total COGS** | **$2,450** | **$3,400** | **$4,700** |
| **Gross profit** | **$550** | **$600** | **$300** |
| **Gross margin** | **18%** | **15%** | **6%** |
| Effective hourly rate | $188 | $182 | $167 |

**Critical observation:** At $150/hr founder opportunity cost, SMB project margins are thin (6-24%). This is structurally different from mid-market, where the effective rate reaches $200-500+/hr. The SMB lane becomes genuinely profitable under two conditions:

1. **The founder's true hourly cost is lower** — if the founder would otherwise be idle (no mid-market pipeline), the opportunity cost is $0, and SMB margins jump to 95%+. SMB work is high-margin when it fills gaps between larger projects.
2. **A junior hire or contractor delivers the work** — at $40-75/hr loaded cost for a junior AI automation specialist, margins transform:

#### SMB Economics with Junior Hire/Contractor ($50/hr)

| Project Type | Revenue (Midpoint) | Junior Hours | Junior Cost | API/Tools | Founder QA (1-2 hrs) | Total COGS | Gross Profit | Gross Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Simple | $1,000 | 5 hrs | $250 | $15 | $225 | $490 | $510 | **51%** |
| Standard | $2,250 | 12 hrs | $600 | $40 | $300 | $940 | $1,310 | **58%** |
| Complex | $4,000 | 22 hrs | $1,100 | $100 | $375 | $1,575 | $2,425 | **61%** |

**This is the target operating model.** The founder discovers, scopes, and quality-checks; a junior delivers. Margins reach 50-61% — comparable to mid-market Build margins with contractors.

### Support Retainer Economics

| Tier | Monthly Revenue | Actual Hours/Mo (Avg) | Founder Cost (@$150/hr) | API/Tools | Gross Profit | Gross Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Basic ($200/mo) | $200 | 0.5-1 hrs | $113 | $5 | $82 | **41%** |
| Standard ($350/mo) | $350 | 1-1.5 hrs | $188 | $10 | $152 | **43%** |
| Premium ($500/mo) | $500 | 2-3 hrs | $375 | $20 | $105 | **21%** |

**With junior hire ($50/hr):**

| Tier | Monthly Revenue | Junior Hours | Junior Cost | Founder QA | Total COGS | Gross Profit | Gross Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Basic | $200 | 0.75 hrs | $38 | $0 | $43 | $157 | **79%** |
| Standard | $350 | 1.25 hrs | $63 | $0 | $73 | $277 | **79%** |
| Premium | $500 | 2.5 hrs | $125 | $75 | $220 | $280 | **56%** |

Support retainers are highly profitable once a junior handles them. A portfolio of 15 SMB support clients at an average of $300/mo = $4,500/mo in revenue at ~75% margin, requiring ~20 hours/mo of junior time.

### Capacity Analysis: How Many SMB Clients Per Month?

**Scenario A: Founder delivers SMB (no junior hire)**

| Assumption | Value |
|---|---|
| Available weekly hours | 40-45 |
| Mid-market allocation (primary lane) | 25-30 hrs/week |
| Remaining for SMB | 10-15 hrs/week = 40-60 hrs/month |
| Average SMB project hours (standard automation) | 12 hrs |
| SMB projects per month | 3-5 |
| Average project revenue | $2,250 |
| Monthly SMB revenue | $6,750-$11,250 |
| Monthly SMB gross profit (at founder cost) | $1,215-$2,025 |

**Scenario B: Junior hire delivers SMB (founder scopes + QA only)**

| Assumption | Value |
|---|---|
| Junior hours available | 120-140 hrs/month (30-35 hrs/week, part-time to full-time) |
| Average SMB project hours (junior) | 12 hrs |
| SMB projects per month | 8-10 |
| Founder hours per SMB project (scope + QA) | 2 hrs |
| Founder hours consumed on SMB | 16-20 hrs/month |
| Average project revenue | $2,250 |
| Monthly SMB revenue | $18,000-$22,500 |
| Monthly SMB gross profit (with junior at $50/hr) | $10,440-$13,050 |

**Scenario B is the target by Month 12-18.** The founder invests 16-20 hrs/month in scoping and QA, while a junior delivers 8-10 projects, generating $10-13K/mo in gross profit from the SMB lane alone.

### The Equivalence Math: SMB vs. Mid-Market

| Metric | 1 Mid-Market Build | Equivalent SMB Volume |
|---|---|---|
| Revenue | $70,000 | 28 standard automations at $2,500 each |
| Founder hours | 80-200 hrs | 56 hrs scope/QA + junior delivers (Scenario B) |
| Delivery timeline | 6-8 weeks | Spread across 3-4 months |
| Gross profit | $38,800-$41,800 | ~$16,240 (at junior delivery margins) |
| Client relationships | 1 | 28 (potential referral sources) |
| Cash flow pattern | Lumpy (milestone-based) | Steady (weekly/biweekly payments) |

**SMB does not replace mid-market — it complements it.** One mid-market Build is worth 2-3 months of solid SMB work in gross profit. But SMB provides something mid-market cannot: steady weekly cash flow and a large referral network.

---

## Go-to-Market for Local Businesses

The SMB go-to-market is fundamentally different from mid-market. Mid-market clients are found via LinkedIn, content marketing, and professional networks. Local businesses are found where local businesses already gather — in person, on local platforms, and through trusted intermediaries.

### Channel 1: Local Networking (Highest Priority)

**Chamber of Commerce:**
- Join the local Chamber ($200-500/year). Attend monthly meetings and mixers.
- Offer to present a 15-minute "How AI Can Save Your Business 10 Hours/Week" talk at a meeting. Chambers are always looking for speakers with practical content.
- Target: Meet 5-10 business owners per event. Convert 1-2 to discovery calls per event.
- Many metro areas have multiple Chambers (city, county, industry-specific). Join 1-2 that concentrate service businesses.

**BNI (Business Network International) and Similar Referral Groups:**
- BNI chapters meet weekly. Each member is expected to pass referrals to other members. Annual membership: $500-1,000.
- Ideal because: every BNI member is a small business owner, they already believe in investing in their business, and the weekly meeting creates consistent exposure.
- The "AI automation specialist" seat in a BNI chapter is almost certainly unfilled — this is a new category.
- Target: 2-4 referrals per month after a 2-3 month warm-up period.

**Trade Associations and Local Guilds:**
- Plumbing associations, HVAC trade groups, restaurant associations, salon owner networks — every trade has a local or regional association.
- Attend quarterly meetings, sponsor a lunch, or offer a free workshop.
- Target: 1-2 discovery calls per event/association.

**Expected volume from local networking (combined):** 4-8 discovery calls/month by Month 3.

### Channel 2: Word of Mouth and Referral Programs

**Why it matters more than any other channel:** Small business owners trust other small business owners. A plumber's recommendation to another plumber is worth more than any ad or LinkedIn post. Studies consistently show that 85% of small businesses cite word-of-mouth as their top source of new business relationships.

**Structured referral program:**

| Incentive | Structure | Target |
|---|---|---|
| **Referral discount** | Referring client gets $200 off next month's support or next project | Simple, easy to track |
| **Cash referral bonus** | $250 cash for every referral that becomes a paid client | More motivating for businesses not on retainer |
| **Free enhancement** | Referring client gets a free automation enhancement (value: $300-500) | Deepens the relationship and demonstrates more capability |
| **Reciprocal referral** | You refer your clients to their business; they refer their contacts to you | Especially effective with complementary businesses (accountants, business coaches) |

**Viral mechanics in local business communities:**
- Trades cluster geographically and socially. Plumbers know plumbers. HVAC techs attend the same supply house. Salon owners go to the same trade shows.
- One successful project in a trade creates a beachhead for that trade in that metro area.
- Target: 1-3 referrals per happy client, with a 30% conversion rate on referred leads (higher than cold because pre-qualified by trust).

### Channel 3: Local Digital Presence

**Facebook Groups and Nextdoor:**
- Local business owner Facebook groups (e.g., "[City] Small Business Owners," "[City] Entrepreneurs") have 500-10,000+ members in most metro areas.
- Nextdoor has a "Local Business" section and neighborhood-level targeting.
- Tactic: Provide genuinely helpful advice about automation in these groups. Share (anonymized) before/after results. Do not hard-sell.
- Post format that works: "I just helped a [trade] business in [city] save 12 hours/week by automating their [specific process]. Happy to answer questions about what's possible with AI for local businesses."
- Target: 2-4 discovery call requests/month from digital presence by Month 4.

**Google Business Profile + Local SEO:**
- Create a Google Business Profile optimized for "AI automation [city]," "business automation consultant [city]," "AI consultant for small business [city]."
- Local SEO for these terms is low-competition — there are almost no local AI automation consultants targeting these keywords.
- Collect Google reviews from every SMB client. Local businesses check reviews before engaging any service provider.
- Target: 1-3 inbound leads/month by Month 6 (local SEO takes 3-6 months to build).

**Simple website/landing page:**
- A dedicated SMB page on the main website: "AI Automation for [City] Businesses"
- Use case examples by business type (plumbing, salons, restaurants, etc.)
- Social proof: testimonials and before/after metrics
- Clear CTA: "Book a free 30-minute discovery call"

### Channel 4: Strategic Partnerships

**Accountants and Bookkeepers:**
- They already serve every small business. They see their clients' operational inefficiencies in the financial data (high labor costs, missed revenue, cash flow problems).
- Offer a referral partnership: accountant refers clients who could benefit from automation, you pay a $200-300 referral fee or provide a reciprocal referral flow.
- Target: 2-3 accountant partnerships, generating 1-2 referrals/month each.

**Business Coaches and Local Consultants:**
- Small business coaches, SCORE mentors, and local management consultants often identify operational problems but lack the technical ability to solve them.
- Position as their "implementation arm" — they diagnose the business problem, you build the AI solution.
- Target: 1-2 active partnerships by Month 4.

**Insurance Agents and Commercial Brokers:**
- They interact with hundreds of local businesses. They can introduce you in exchange for reciprocal referrals.
- Low-effort partnership: provide them with a one-pager they can share with clients.

**IT Service Providers / MSPs:**
- Local managed IT companies serve small businesses but typically do not offer AI automation.
- White-label or referral partnership: they refer automation needs, you deliver, they maintain the relationship.
- Target: 1 MSP partnership generating 2-3 referrals/month.

### Channel 5: Workshops and Lunch-and-Learns

**Format:** A 45-60 minute workshop titled something like:
- "How AI Can Save Your [Trade] Business 10 Hours/Week"
- "3 Things Every [City] Business Should Automate in 2026"
- "AI for Local Businesses: What Actually Works (and What's Hype)"

**Venues:**
- Chamber of Commerce meeting rooms
- Local co-working spaces
- Trade association meetings
- Library community rooms (free)
- Partner with a restaurant (they provide the space and food, you mention them)

**Economics:**
- Cost: $0-200 (room rental, light refreshments)
- Attendees: 10-25 per workshop
- Conversion: 15-25% book a discovery call
- Discovery calls from 1 workshop: 2-5

**Frequency:** 1 workshop per month. Can batch-prepare the content and adapt for different audiences (trades, restaurants, professional services).

### Channel 6: Industry-Specific Online Communities

**Trade forums and Facebook groups (national):**
- Facebook groups like "HVAC Technicians," "Plumbing Contractors," "Salon Owners Network" have tens of thousands of members.
- Reddit communities: r/HVAC, r/Plumbing, r/smallbusiness, r/restaurateur
- Tactic: Provide value, share results, answer questions. Link to a free discovery call for interested parties.
- Advantage: Scales beyond local geography. Can serve SMBs remotely (most automations do not require on-site work).

### Channel Summary and Expected Volume

| Channel | Monthly Discovery Calls (Steady State) | Ramp Time | Cost/Month |
|---|---|---|---|
| Local networking (Chamber, BNI, associations) | 4-8 | 2-3 months | $100-200 (dues, meals) |
| Word of mouth / referrals | 2-4 | 3-6 months | $200-400 (referral incentives) |
| Local digital (Facebook groups, Nextdoor, Google) | 2-4 | 3-6 months | $0-100 |
| Strategic partnerships | 2-4 | 3-4 months | $200-400 (referral fees) |
| Workshops | 2-5 (monthly event) | 1-2 months | $0-200 per event |
| Online trade communities | 1-3 | 2-4 months | $0 |
| **Total** | **13-28** | — | **$500-1,300/mo** |

**Conversion targets:**
- Discovery call to paid project: 30-40% (higher than mid-market because shorter sales cycle and lower price = less deliberation)
- At 15-20 discovery calls/month with 35% conversion: **5-7 new SMB clients/month**

---

## Operational Model: Two-Lane Strategy

### Time Allocation

The two-lane model must be structured to prevent the SMB lane from consuming the founder's highest-leverage hours. Mid-market work is higher-margin and higher-LTV per hour of founder time; SMB generates volume and cash flow stability.

#### Phase 1: Founder Does Both (Months 1-12)

| Activity | Hours/Week | Lane |
|---|---|---|
| Mid-market client work (discovery, builds, support) | 20-25 | Mid-market |
| Mid-market sales (scans, proposals, follow-up) | 5-8 | Mid-market |
| SMB client work (discovery, builds, support) | 8-12 | SMB |
| SMB marketing (networking, events, partnerships) | 3-5 | SMB |
| Admin, professional development, overhead | 3-5 | Both |
| **Total** | **39-55** | |

**Caution:** At the upper end (55 hrs/week), this is not sustainable. The realistic maximum during Phase 1 is 3-4 SMB projects per month alongside mid-market obligations. The founder must resist the temptation to "just take one more" SMB project.

**Priority rule:** When mid-market and SMB compete for the same hours, mid-market wins. Always. A mid-market Build at $70,000 generates more profit than 10 SMB standard automations at $2,250 each. SMB fills gaps in the schedule; it does not displace mid-market work.

#### Phase 2: Junior Hire Handles SMB Delivery (Months 12-18+)

| Activity | Hours/Week | Who |
|---|---|---|
| Mid-market client work | 20-25 | Founder |
| Mid-market sales | 5-8 | Founder |
| SMB scoping + QA | 4-6 | Founder |
| SMB delivery | 25-30 | Junior hire |
| SMB marketing | 2-3 | Founder (can share with junior) |
| SMB client communication | 5-8 | Junior hire |
| Admin, professional development | 3-5 | Founder |
| **Total (Founder)** | **34-47** | |
| **Total (Junior)** | **30-38** | |

**Hire trigger:** The SMB lane justifies a junior hire when:
- SMB revenue exceeds $8,000/mo for 3 consecutive months
- There is a consistent pipeline of 4+ SMB leads per month
- The founder is turning away SMB work due to capacity constraints
- The hire's loaded cost ($4,000-5,500/mo for part-time to full-time junior) is covered by SMB margin with room to spare

### Batching SMB Work

SMB projects can be batched for efficiency:

| Strategy | How It Works | Efficiency Gain |
|---|---|---|
| **Industry batching** | Do 3-4 plumber automations in a row. Reuse 60-70% of the architecture, customize 30-40%. | Reduces per-project time by 30-40% after the first build in a trade |
| **Day batching** | Reserve specific days for SMB work (e.g., Tuesdays and Thursdays). Mid-market on other days. | Reduces context-switching overhead; protects mid-market flow |
| **Discovery batching** | Schedule all SMB discovery calls on one day (e.g., Tuesday mornings) | Efficient use of "sales mode" time |
| **Delivery sprints** | Accumulate 3-4 scoped SMB projects, then deliver in a 2-3 day sprint | Flow state on similar work; faster completion |

### Contractor Model for SMB

Before a full junior hire, a contractor can handle SMB delivery:

| Model | Cost | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Per-project contractor** ($30-50/hr offshore, $50-75/hr domestic) | $150-600 per simple automation; $400-1,500 per standard | No fixed cost; pay only when projects exist | Quality variance; onboarding time per contractor |
| **Retained contractor** (10-20 hrs/week at $40-60/hr) | $1,600-$4,800/mo | Consistent quality; builds SMB expertise | Fixed cost even if pipeline dips |
| **Part-time junior hire** (20 hrs/week) | $2,000-3,500/mo | Deepest integration; trains into the role | Highest fixed cost; management overhead |

**Recommended progression:**
1. Months 1-6: Founder delivers all SMB work personally (learning the patterns)
2. Months 6-9: Per-project contractor for overflow, founder still does discovery and QA
3. Months 9-12: Move to a retained contractor (10-15 hrs/week) if volume supports it
4. Months 12-18: Evaluate part-time or full-time junior hire based on consistent volume

### Impact on Break-Even

From Module 6, the base case monthly break-even (fixed costs + personal draw) is $6,650-$12,200 depending on the month.

| Scenario | Break-Even Month (Mid-Market Only) | Break-Even Month (Combined) | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conservative | Month 5 | Month 3-4 | 1-2 months earlier |
| Base Case | Month 3 | Month 2 | 1 month earlier |
| Optimistic | Month 1 | Month 1 | Same (already immediate) |

**The primary financial benefit of the SMB lane is not earlier break-even — it is cash flow smoothing.** Mid-market revenue is lumpy: a $70K Build pays in 2-3 milestone installments over 6-8 weeks, followed by potentially weeks of zero project revenue while the next deal closes. SMB revenue arrives weekly in small, steady increments. This smoothing reduces the cash reserves needed to weather gaps between mid-market projects.

### SMB Go-to-Market Costs

| Item | Monthly Cost | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Chamber of Commerce dues | $25-50 | Annual dues amortized monthly |
| BNI membership | $50-80 | Annual dues amortized monthly |
| Referral incentives | $200-400 | 1-2 referral payouts/month |
| Workshop costs | $0-200 | 1 per month; room rental, refreshments |
| Google Business / local SEO | $0-50 | DIY initially |
| **Total SMB GTM cost** | **$275-780/mo** | |

These are modest compared to the revenue they generate. At base-case SMB revenue of $10,000-20,000/mo, the GTM spend is 1.5-8% of SMB revenue — well within healthy ranges.
